# MDMA

For other uses, see [MDMA (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDMA_\(disambiguation\)). Not to be confused with [MDA (drug)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDA_\(drug\)); [EDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EDMA); [NMDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NMDA); [2,3-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2,3-MDMA); [MDNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDNA); or [NDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NDMA).

**3,4-Methylenedioxymethamphetamine** (**MDMA**), commonly known as **ecstasy** in tablet form, and **molly** in crystal form,[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-22] is an [entactogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entactogen) with [stimulant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stimulant) and minor [psychedelic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_drug) properties.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Green_2003-23][^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

MDMA was first synthesized in 1912 by [Merck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merck_Group) chemist [Anton Köllisch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_K%C3%B6llisch).[^cite_note-FreudenmannÖxlerBernschneider-Reif2006-25] It was used to enhance psychotherapy beginning in the 1970s and became popular as a street drug in the 1980s.[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26][^cite_note-NIH2016-27] MDMA is commonly used by attendees of [dance parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_party), [raves](43488.md), and [electronic dance music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music) festivals.[^cite_note-WHO2004-28] Tablets sold as ecstasy may be [mixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cutting_agent) with other substances such as [ephedrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephedrine), [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine), and [methamphetamine](11038318.md).[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26]

The lethal risk from one dose of MDMA is estimated to be from 1 death in 20,000 instances to 1 death in 50,000 instances.[^cite_note-White_2014-29]

The purported pharmacological effects that may be [prosocial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosocial_behavior) include altered sensations, increased energy, empathy, and pleasure.[^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24][^cite_note-Drugs2014-26] When taken by mouth, effects begin in 30 to 45 minutes and last three to six hours.[^cite_note-Freye_2009-14][^cite_note-NIH2016-27] Short-term adverse effects include [bruxism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruxism), [blurred vision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blurred_vision), [sweating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweating), and [tachycardia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tachycardia),[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26] and extended use can also lead to addiction, [amnesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesia), [memory disorders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memory_disorder), [paranoia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paranoia), and [insomnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insomnia). Deaths have been reported due to increased body temperature and dehydration. MDMA acts primarily by increasing the release of the [neurotransmitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurotransmitters) [serotonin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin), [dopamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dopamine), and [norepinephrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norepinephrine) in parts of the brain.[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26][^cite_note-NIH2016-27] It belongs to the [substituted amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_amphetamine) [classes of drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_classification).[^cite_note-EU2015-10][^cite_note-30] MDMA is structurally similar to [mescaline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mescaline) (a psychedelic), methamphetamine (a stimulant), as well as [endogenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endogeny_\(biology\)) [monoamine neurotransmitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_neurotransmitter) such as serotonin, norepinephrine, and dopamine.[^cite_note-31]

MDMA has limited approved medical uses in a small number of countries,[^cite_note-32] but is illegal in most jurisdictions.[^cite_note-33] [MDMA-assisted psychotherapy](60912660.md) may substantially improve PTSD symptoms, response, and remission rates compared to psychotherapy alone, but the evidence is low to very low certainty and safety data are limited, with some increased transient adverse events.[^cite_note-Yong_2025-34] In the United States, the [Food and Drug Administration](11632.md) (FDA) has given MDMA [breakthrough therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakthrough_therapy) status (though there are no current clinical indications in the US).[^cite_note-35] Canada has allowed limited distribution of MDMA upon application to and approval by [Health Canada](216516.md).[^cite_note-36] In Australia, it may be prescribed in the treatment of PTSD by specifically authorised psychiatrists.[^cite_note-37]

## Uses

### Recreational

MDMA is often considered the drug of choice within the [rave](43488.md) culture and is also used at clubs, festivals, and [house parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_party).[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16] In the rave environment, the sensory effects of music and lighting are often highly [synergistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synergy) with the drug. The psychedelic [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine) quality of MDMA offers multiple appealing aspects to users in the rave setting. Some users enjoy the feeling of mass communion from the inhibition-reducing effects of the drug, while others use it as party fuel because of the drug's stimulatory effects.[^cite_note-38] MDMA is used less often than other stimulants, typically less than once per week.[^cite_note-Epstein2013-39]

MDMA is sometimes taken in conjunction with other psychoactive drugs such as [LSD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LSD),[^cite_note-\:0-40] [psilocybin mushrooms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psilocybin_mushroom), [2C-B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2C-B), and [ketamine](16948.md). The combination with LSD is called "candy-flipping".[^cite_note-\:0-40] The combination with 2C-B is called "nexus flipping". For this combination, most people take the MDMA first, wait until the peak is over, and then take the 2C-B.[^cite_note-41]

MDMA is often co-administered with [alcohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcohol_\(drug\)), [methamphetamine](11038318.md), and prescription drugs such as [SSRIs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selective_serotonin_reuptake_inhibitor) with which MDMA has several drug-drug interactions.[^cite_note-Hysek_2012-42][^cite_note-43][^cite_note-44] Three life-threatening reports of MDMA co-administration with [ritonavir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritonavir) have been reported;[^cite_note-45] with ritonavir having severe and dangerous drug-drug interactions with a wide range of both psychoactive, anti-psychotic, and non-psychoactive drugs.[^cite_note-Carpenter_2019-46]

### Medical

See also: [MDMA § Research](#Research), and [MDMA-assisted psychotherapy](60912660.md)

Before it was widely banned, it saw limited use in psychotherapy.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-EU2015-10][^cite_note-47] In 2017 the [United States Food and Drug Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Food_and_Drug_Administration) (FDA) granted breakthrough therapy designation for MDMA-assisted psychotherapy for [post-traumatic stress disorder](82974.md) (PTSD).[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49]

MDMA has limited approved medical uses in a small number of countries,[^cite_note-50] but is illegal in most jurisdictions.[^cite_note-51] Canada has allowed limited distribution of MDMA upon application to and approval by [Health Canada](216516.md).[^cite_note-52] In Australia, it may be prescribed in the treatment of PTSD by specifically authorised psychiatrists.[^cite_note-53]

Fourteen systematic reviews of up to 353 participants suggest MDMA-assisted psychotherapy may substantially improve PTSD symptoms, response, and remission rates compared to psychotherapy alone, but the evidence is low to very low certainty and safety data are limited, with some increased transient adverse events.[^cite_note-Yong_2025-34]

Some researchers have proposed that psychedelics in general may act as [active](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_placebo) "super [placebos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placebo)" used for therapeutic purposes.[^cite_note-Dupuis_2022-54][^cite_note-van_Elk_2022-55]

### Others

Small doses of MDMA are used by some [religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion) practitioners as an [entheogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entheogen) to enhance [prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prayer) or [meditation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meditation).[^cite_note-MDMA_and_Religion-56] MDMA has been used as an adjunct to [New Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Age) spiritual practices.[^cite_note-Watson_&_Beck-57]

### Forms



1 gram of pure MDMA hydrochloride crystals

MDMA has become widely known as ecstasy (shortened "E", "X", or "XTC"), usually referring to its tablet form, although this term may also include the presence of possible [adulterants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adulterant) or diluents. The UK term "mandy" and the US term "molly" colloquially refer to MDMA in a crystalline powder form that is thought to be free of adulterants.[^cite_note-Luciano_2014-2][^cite_note-DrugFacts-3][^cite_note-DEA_2015_assessment-58] MDMA is also sold in the form of the hydrochloride salt, either as loose crystals or in [gelcaps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capsule_\(pharmacy\)).[^cite_note-Molly_S05E07-59][^cite_note-Molly_S06E05-60] MDMA tablets can sometimes be found in a shaped form that may depict characters from [popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_culture). These are sometimes collectively referred to as "fun tablets".[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62]

Partly due to the global supply shortage of [sassafras oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sassafras_oil)—a problem largely assuaged by use of improved or alternative modern methods of synthesis—the purity of substances sold as molly have been found to vary widely. Some of these substances contain [methylone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylone), [ethylone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethylone), [MDPV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDPV), [mephedrone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mephedrone), or any other of the group of compounds commonly known as [bath salts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath_salts_\(drug\)), in addition to, or in place of, MDMA.[^cite_note-DrugFacts-3][^cite_note-DEA_2015_assessment-58][^cite_note-Molly_S05E07-59][^cite_note-Molly_S06E05-60] Powdered MDMA ranges from pure MDMA to crushed tablets with 30–40% purity.[^cite_note-EU2015-10] MDMA tablets typically have low purity due to bulking agents that are added to dilute the drug and increase profits (notably lactose) and binding agents.[^cite_note-EU2015-10] Tablets sold as ecstasy sometimes contain [3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine) (MDA), [3,4-methylenedioxyethylamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-methylenedioxyethylamphetamine) (MDEA), other amphetamine derivatives, caffeine, opiates, or painkillers.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] Some tablets contain little or no MDMA.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-EU2015-10][^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] The proportion of seized ecstasy tablets with MDMA-like impurities has varied annually and by country.[^cite_note-EU2015-10] The average content of MDMA in a preparation is 70 to 120 mg with the purity having increased since the 1990s.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

MDMA is usually consumed by mouth. It is also sometimes [snorted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insufflation_\(medicine\)).[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26]

## Effects and side effects



Pupils dilated from MDMA use.

### Effects

In general, MDMA users report feeling the onset of subjective effects within 30 to 60 minutes of oral consumption and reaching peak effect at 75 to 120 minutes, which then plateaus for about 3.5 hours.[^cite_note-mcn-64] The desired short-term psychoactive effects of MDMA have been reported to include:

- [Euphoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphoria)/[ecstasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecstasy_\(emotion\)) – a sense of general [well-being](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Well-being) and [happiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Happiness)

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- Increased self-confidence, sociability, and perception of facilitated communication

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- [Entactogenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entactogen) effects—increased [empathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empathy) or feelings of closeness with others

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

  &#x20;and oneself

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

- [Dilated pupils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mydriasis)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

- Relaxation and reduced anxiety

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

- Increased emotionality

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

- A sense of inner peace

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- Mild hallucination

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- Enhanced sensation, perception, or sexuality

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- Altered sense of time

  [^cite_note-NIH2016-27]

The experience elicited by MDMA depends on the dose, setting, and user.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] The variability of the induced altered state is lower compared to other psychedelics. For example, MDMA used at parties is associated with high motor activity, reduced sense of identity, and poor awareness of surroundings. Use of MDMA individually or in small groups in a quiet environment and when concentrating, is associated with increased lucidity, concentration, sensitivity to aesthetic aspects of the environment, enhanced awareness of emotions, and improved capability of communication.[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Landriscina-66] In psychotherapeutic settings, MDMA effects have been characterized by infantile ideas, mood lability, and memories and moods connected with childhood experiences.[^cite_note-Landriscina-66][^cite_note-Baggott_2015-67]

MDMA has been described as an "[empathogenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empathogen)" drug because of its empathy-producing effects.[^cite_note-Schmid_2014-68][^cite_note-Wardle_2014-69] Results of several studies show the effects of increased empathy with others.[^cite_note-Schmid_2014-68] When testing MDMA for medium and high doses, it showed increased hedonic and arousal continuum.[^cite_note-Bravo_2001-70][^cite_note-Metzner_2005-71] The effect of MDMA increasing sociability is consistent, while its effects on empathy have been more mixed.[^cite_note-KamilarBritt_2015-72]

### Side effects

#### Short-term

Acute adverse effects are usually the result of high or multiple doses, although single dose toxicity can occur in susceptible individuals.[^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24] The most serious short-term physical health risks of MDMA are [hyperthermia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperthermia) and [dehydration](155056.md).[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Keane_2014-73] Cases of life-threatening or fatal [hyponatremia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyponatremia) (excessively low sodium concentration in the blood) have developed in MDMA users attempting to prevent dehydration by [consuming excessive amounts of water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_intoxication) without replenishing [electrolytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrolytes).[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Keane_2014-73][^cite_note-White_2014-29]

The immediate adverse effects of MDMA use can include:

- [Bruxism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruxism) (grinding and clenching of the teeth)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-pmid22392347-16]

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

- [Dehydration](155056.md)

  [^cite_note-pmid22392347-16]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

  [^cite_note-Keane_2014-73]

- [Diarrhea](53951.md)

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- [Erectile dysfunction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erectile_dysfunction)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-74]

- [Hyperthermia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperthermia)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-pmid22392347-16]

  [^cite_note-Keane_2014-73]

- Increased wakefulness or [insomnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insomnia)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

- Increased perspiration and sweating

  [^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65]

  [^cite_note-Keane_2014-73]

- Increased [heart rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_rate) and [blood pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_pressure)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-pmid22392347-16]

  [^cite_note-Keane_2014-73]

- Increased [psychomotor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychomotor_agitation) activity

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

- Loss of [appetite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appetite)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Nausea and vomiting

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

- Visual and auditory hallucinations (rarely)

  [^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

Other adverse effects that may occur or persist for up to a week following cessation of moderate MDMA use include:[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63][^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

#### Physiological

- Insomnia

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Loss of appetite

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Tiredness or lethargy

  [^cite_note-75]

  [^cite_note-76]

- [Trismus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trismus) (lockjaw)

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

#### Psychological

- [Anhedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhedonia)

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Anxiety or paranoia

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Depression

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

- Impulsiveness

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Irritability

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

- Memory impairment

  [^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24]

- Restlessness

  [^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63]

#### Long-term

There is consistent evidence of structural and functional deficits in MDMA users with high lifetime exposure.[^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77] These structural or functional changes appear to be dose dependent and may be less prominent in MDMA users with a lifetime exposure of less than 50 doses used and less than 100 tablets consumed. Nonetheless, moderate MDMA use may still result in [neurotoxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurotoxicity) and what constitutes moderate use is not clearly established.[^cite_note-Mueller_2016-78]

Furthermore, it is not clear yet whether "typical" recreational users of MDMA (1 to 2 pills of 75 to 125 mg MDMA or analogue every 1 to 4 weeks) will develop neurotoxic brain lesions.[^cite_note-79] Long-term exposure to MDMA in humans has been shown to produce marked [neurodegeneration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurodegeneration) in [striatal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striatal), [hippocampal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippocampal), [prefrontal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefrontal_cortex), and [occipital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occipital_cortex) serotonergic [axon terminals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axon_terminal).[^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77][^cite_note-Meth_MDMA_NTox-80] Neurotoxic damage to serotonergic axon terminals has been shown to persist for more than two years.[^cite_note-Meth_MDMA_NTox-80] Elevations in brain temperature from MDMA use are positively correlated with MDMA-induced neurotoxicity.[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77][^cite_note-Mueller_2016-78] However, most studies on MDMA and serotonergic neurotoxicity in humans focus more on heavy users who consume as much as seven times or more the amount that most users report taking. The evidence for the presence of serotonergic neurotoxicity in casual users who take lower doses less frequently is not conclusive.[^cite_note-81]

However, adverse [neuroplastic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuroplastic) changes to brain [microvasculature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microvasculature) and [white matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_matter) have been observed to occur in humans using low doses of MDMA.[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77] Reduced [gray matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_matter) density in certain brain structures has also been noted in human MDMA users.[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77] Global reductions in gray matter volume, thinning of the parietal and orbitofrontal cortices, and decreased hippocampal activity have been observed in long term users.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] The effects established so far for recreational use of ecstasy lie in the range of moderate to severe effects for [serotonin transporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_transporter) reduction.[^cite_note-82]

Impairments in multiple aspects of cognition, including attention, learning, memory, [visual processing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Role_of_serotonin_in_visual_orientation_processing#MDMA_and_Visual_Orientation_Processing), and sleep, have been found in regular MDMA users.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24][^cite_note-Pharm2014-83][^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77] The magnitude of these impairments is correlated with lifetime MDMA usage[^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24][^cite_note-Pharm2014-83][^cite_note-Abstinent_MDMA_fMRI_review-77] and are partially reversible with abstinence.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] Several forms of memory are impaired by chronic ecstasy use;[^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24][^cite_note-Pharm2014-83] however, the effects for memory impairments in ecstasy users are generally small overall.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-Meta_analysis_-_MDMA_memory_impairment-85] MDMA use is also associated with increased impulsivity and depression.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

Serotonin depletion following MDMA use can cause depression in subsequent days. In some cases, depressive symptoms persist for longer periods.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] Some studies indicate repeated recreational use of ecstasy is associated with depression and anxiety, even after quitting the drug.[^cite_note-86] Depression is one of the main reasons for cessation of use.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

At high doses, MDMA induces a response in the [neuroimmune system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuroimmune_system) that, through several mechanisms, increases the permeability of the [blood–brain barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood%E2%80%93brain_barrier), thereby making the brain more susceptible to environmental toxins and [pathogens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathogen).[^cite_note-MDMA_BBB-87][^cite_note-88] In addition, MDMA has [immunosuppressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immunosuppressive) effects in the [peripheral nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peripheral_nervous_system) and pro-inflammatory effects in the [central nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_nervous_system).[^cite_note-89]

MDMA may increase the risk of [cardiac valvulopathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_valvulopathy) in heavy or long-term users due to activation of serotonin [5-HT<sub>2B</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2B_receptor).[^cite_note-Cavero_2014-90][^cite_note-Padhariya_2017-91] MDMA induces cardiac [epigenetic changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epigenetics) in [DNA methylation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNA_methylation), particularly hypermethylation changes.[^cite_note-92]

&#x20;Research links regular MDMA use to reduced [grey matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_matter) volume, particularly in the [hippocampus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippocampus). Structural [MRI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRI) scans show significant volume reductions in the hippocampus, specifically subregions such as CA1, which directly control memory formation.[^cite_note-93]

#### Reinforcement disorders

Approximately 60% of MDMA users experience [drug withdrawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_withdrawal) symptoms when they stop taking MDMA.[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] Some of these symptoms include fatigue, loss of appetite, depression, and trouble concentrating.[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] [Tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_tolerance) to some of the desired and adverse effects of MDMA is expected to occur with consistent MDMA use.[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] A 2007 [delphic analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi_method) of a panel of experts in pharmacology, psychiatry, law, policing and others estimated MDMA to have a psychological dependence and physical dependence potential roughly three-fourths to four-fifths that of cannabis.[^cite_note-Nutt_2007-94]

MDMA has been shown to induce [ΔFosB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%CE%94FosB) in the [nucleus accumbens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nucleus_accumbens).[^cite_note-MDMA_ΔFosB-95] Because MDMA releases dopamine in the [striatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striatum), the mechanisms by which it induces ΔFosB in the nucleus accumbens are analogous to other dopaminergic psychostimulants.[^cite_note-MDMA_ΔFosB-95][^cite_note-Robison_2011-96] Therefore, chronic use of MDMA at high doses can result in [altered brain structure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuroplasticity) and [drug addiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_addiction) that occur as a consequence of ΔFosB overexpression in the nucleus accumbens.[^cite_note-Robison_2011-96] MDMA is less addictive than other stimulants such as methamphetamine and cocaine.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] Compared with amphetamine, MDMA and its metabolite MDA are less reinforcing.[^cite_note-99]

One study found approximately 15% of chronic MDMA users met the [DSM-IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DSM-IV) diagnostic criteria for [substance dependence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substance_dependence).[^cite_note-Steinkellner_2011-100] However, there is little evidence for a specific diagnosable MDMA dependence syndrome because MDMA is typically used relatively infrequently.[^cite_note-Epstein2013-39]

There are currently no medications to treat MDMA addiction.[^cite_note-101]

#### During pregnancy

MDMA is a moderately [teratogenic drug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teratogenic_drug) (i.e., it is toxic to the fetus).[^cite_note-Vorhees_1997-102][^cite_note-Meamar_2010-103] [In utero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uterus) exposure to MDMA is associated with a [neuro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurotoxicity)- and [cardiotoxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiotoxicity)[^cite_note-Meamar_2010-103] and impaired motor functioning. Motor delays may be temporary during infancy or long-term. The severity of these developmental delays increases with heavier MDMA use.[^cite_note-Pharm2014-83][^cite_note-singer-104] MDMA has been shown to promote the survival of fetal dopaminergic neurons in culture.[^cite_note-105]

## Overdose

MDMA [overdose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overdose) symptoms vary widely due to the involvement of multiple [organ systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organ_system). Some of the more overt overdose symptoms are listed in the table below. The number of instances of fatal MDMA intoxication is low relative to its usage rates. In most fatalities, MDMA was not the only drug involved. Acute toxicity is mainly caused by [serotonin syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_syndrome) and [sympathomimetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sympathomimetic) effects.[^cite_note-Steinkellner_2011-100] MDMA's toxicity in overdose may be exacerbated by [caffeine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caffeine), with which it is frequently cut in order to increase volume.[^cite_note-106] The estimated fatal dose of MDMA in humans is around 15 or 16 times a typical recreational dose.[^cite_note-Henríquez-HernándezRojas-HernándezQuintana-Hernández2023-107] Sympathomimetic side effects can be managed with [carvedilol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carvedilol).[^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Hysek_2012b-109] A scheme for management of acute MDMA toxicity has been published focusing on treatment of hyperthermia, hyponatraemia, serotonin syndrome, and multiple organ failure.[^cite_note-110]

| System | Minor or moderate overdose[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111] | Severe overdose[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111] |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Cardiovascular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiovascular_system) |  | [Disseminated intravascular coagulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disseminated_intravascular_coagulation)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] [Intracranial hemorrhage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intracranial_hemorrhage)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] Severe [hypertension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypertension)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] or [hypotension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypotension)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] Hypotensive bleeding[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8] |
| [Central nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_nervous_system) | [Hyperreflexia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperreflexia)[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111] [Agitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychomotor_agitation)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] [Mental confusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mental_confusion)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] [Paranoia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paranoia)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] [Stimulant psychosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stimulant_psychosis)[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-pmid22392347-16] | [Cognitive and memory impairment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognitive_deficit)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] potentially to the point of [retrograde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrograde_amnesia) or [anterograde amnesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anterograde_amnesia)[^cite_note-113] Coma[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] [Convulsions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convulsion)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] [Hallucinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucination)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Kellum_2008-112] [Loss of consciousness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loss_of_consciousness)[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16] [Serotonin syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_syndrome)[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16][^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-White_2014-29] |
| [Musculoskeletal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musculoskeletal_system) |  | [Muscle rigidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypertonia)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] [Rhabdomyolysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhabdomyolysis) (i.e., rapid muscle breakdown)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-White_2014-29] |
| [Respiratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_system) |  | [Acute respiratory distress syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_respiratory_distress_syndrome)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65] |
| [Urinary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urogenital_system) |  | [Acute kidney injury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_kidney_injury)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-114] |
| Other |  | [Cerebral edema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerebral_edema)[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16] [Hepatitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis)[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-White_2014-29] [Hyperpyrexia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperpyrexia) (a life-threatening elevation of body temperature greater than or equal to 40.0 or 41.5 °C (104.0 or 106.7 °F))[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-White_2014-29] [Hyponatremia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyponatremia) ([syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syndrome_of_inappropriate_antidiuretic_hormone))[^cite_note-Acute_amph_toxicity-65][^cite_note-Keane_2014-73][^cite_note-White_2014-29] |

## Interactions

See also: [Trip killer § Antidotes of other hallucinogens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trip_killer#Antidotes_of_other_hallucinogens), and [MDMA/citalopram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDMA/citalopram)

A number of [drug interactions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_interactions) can occur between MDMA and other drugs, including [serotonergic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin) drugs.[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63][^cite_note-115] MDMA also interacts with drugs which inhibit [CYP450](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CYP450) enzymes, like [ritonavir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritonavir) (Norvir), particularly [CYP2D6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CYP2D6) inhibitors.[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] Life-threatening reactions and death have occurred in people who took MDMA while on ritonavir.[^cite_note-Papaseit_2020-116] [Bupropion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bupropion), a strong CYP2D6 inhibitor, has been found to increase MDMA exposure with administration of MDMA.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Schmid_2015-118] Concurrent use of MDMA with certain other serotonergic drugs can result in a life-threatening condition called [serotonin syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_syndrome).[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] Severe overdose resulting in death has also been reported in people who took MDMA in combination with certain [monoamine oxidase inhibitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_oxidase_inhibitor) (MAOIs),[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8][^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] such as [phenelzine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phenelzine) (Nardil), [tranylcypromine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tranylcypromine) (Parnate), or [moclobemide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moclobemide) (Aurorix, Manerix).[^cite_note-119] [Serotonin reuptake inhibitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_reuptake_inhibitor) (SRIs) such as [citalopram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citalopram) (Celexa), [duloxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duloxetine) (Cymbalta), [fluoxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fluoxetine) (Prozac), and [paroxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paroxetine) (Paxil) have been shown to block most of the subjective effects of MDMA.[^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120] [Norepinephrine reuptake inhibitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norepinephrine_reuptake_inhibitor) (NRIs) such as [reboxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reboxetine) (Edronax) have been found to reduce [emotional excitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emotional_excitation) and feelings of [stimulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychostimulant) with MDMA but do not appear to influence its [entactogenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entactogen) or [mood-elevating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphoriant) effects.[^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120]

MDMA [induces the release of monoamine neurotransmitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_releasing_agent) and thereby acts as an indirectly acting [sympathomimetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sympathomimetic) and produces a variety of [cardiostimulant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiostimulant) effects.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117] It [dose-dependently](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dose_dependence) increases [heart rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_rate), [blood pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_pressure), and [cardiac output](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_output).[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Mendelson_2012-121] SRIs like citalopram and [paroxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paroxetine), as well as the [serotonin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin) [5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2A_receptor) [antagonist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Receptor_antagonist) [ketanserin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ketanserin), have been found to partially block the increases in heart rate and blood pressure with MDMA.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122] It is notable in this regard that [serotonergic psychedelics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonergic_psychedelic) such as [psilocybin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psilocybin), which act as serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor agonists, likewise have sympathomimetic effects.[^cite_note-Wso_2023-123][^cite_note-Neumann_2024-124][^cite_note-Ley_2023-125] The NRI [reboxetine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reboxetine) and the [serotonin–norepinephrine reuptake inhibitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine_reuptake_inhibitor) (SNRI) duloxetine block MDMA-induced increases in heart rate and blood pressure.[^cite_note-VogtLiechti2025-126][^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117] Conversely, bupropion, a [norepinephrine–dopamine reuptake inhibitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norepinephrine%E2%80%93dopamine_reuptake_inhibitor) (NDRI) with only weak [dopaminergic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dopaminergic) activity,[^cite_note-Hart_2024-127][^cite_note-Eap_2021-128] reduced MDMA-induced heart rate and circulating [norepinephrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norepinephrine) increases but did not affect MDMA-induced blood pressure increases.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Schmid_2015-118] On the other hand, the robust NDRI [methylphenidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylphenidate), which has sympathomimetic effects of its own, has been found to augment the cardiovascular effects and increases in circulating norepinephrine and [epinephrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epinephrine) levels induced by MDMA.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Hysek_2014-129]

The [non-selective](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binding_selectivity) [beta blocker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beta_blocker) [pindolol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindolol) blocked MDMA-induced increases in heart rate but not blood pressure.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Hysek_2010-130] The [α<sub>2</sub>-adrenergic receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%CE%912-adrenergic_receptor) [agonist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agonist) [clonidine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clonidine) did not affect the cardiovascular effects of MDMA, though it reduced blood pressure.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Hysek_2012a-131] The [α<sub>1</sub>-adrenergic receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%CE%911-adrenergic_receptor) antagonists [doxazosin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxazosin) and [prazosin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prazosin) blocked or reduced MDMA-induced blood pressure increases but augmented MDMA-induced heart rate and cardiac output increases.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Hysek_2013-132][^cite_note-Mendelson_2012-121] The dual α<sub>1</sub>- and [β-adrenergic receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%CE%92-adrenergic_receptor) blocker [carvedilol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carvedilol) reduced MDMA-induced heart rate and blood pressure increases.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Hysek_2012b-109] In contrast to the cases of serotonergic and noradrenergic agents, the [dopamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dopamine) [D<sub>2</sub> receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D2_receptor) antagonist [haloperidol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haloperidol) did not affect the cardiovascular responses to MDMA.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Liechti_2000a-133] Due to the theoretical risk of "unopposed α-stimulation" and possible consequences like [coronary vasospasm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronary_vasospasm), it has been suggested that dual α<sub>1</sub>- and β-adrenergic receptor antagonists like carvedilol and [labetalol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labetalol), rather than selective beta blockers, should be used in the management of stimulant-induced [sympathomimetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sympathomimetic) [toxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxicity), for instance in the context of [overdose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overdose).[^cite_note-Richards_2015-108][^cite_note-Richards_2017-134]

## Pharmacology

### Pharmacodynamics

See also: [Entactogen § Mechanism of action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entactogen#Mechanism_of_action), [Serotonin releasing agent § Effects and comparisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_releasing_agent#Effects_and_comparisons), and [Monoamine releasing agent § Mechanism of action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_releasing_agent#Mechanism_of_action)

Proteins are human unless otherwise specified. **Refs:** [^cite_note-PDSPKiDatabase-135][^cite_note-BindingDB-136][^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Jain_2025-137][^cite_note-Ray_2010-138][^cite_note-Simmler_2013-139][^cite_note-Simmler_2014-140] [^cite_note-Rickli_2015-141][^cite_note-Luethi_2019-142][^cite_note-Eshleman_2014-143][^cite_note-Gainetdinov_2018-144][^cite_note-Simmler_2016-145][^cite_note-Sotnikova_2009-146] |  |

MDMA is an [entactogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entactogen) or [empathogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empathogen), as well as a [stimulant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stimulant), [euphoriant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphoriant), and weak [psychedelic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_drug).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Nichols_2022-147] It is a [substrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substrate_\(biochemistry\)) of the [monoamine transporters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_transporter) (MATs) and acts as a [monoamine releasing agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_releasing_agent) (MRA).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-Rothman_2003-149][^cite_note-Rothman_2006-150] The drug is specifically a well-balanced [serotonin–norepinephrine–dopamine releasing agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine%E2%80%93dopamine_releasing_agent) (SNDRA).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-Rothman_2003-149][^cite_note-Rothman_2006-150] To a lesser extent, MDMA also acts as a [serotonin–norepinephrine–dopamine reuptake inhibitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine%E2%80%93dopamine_reuptake_inhibitor) (SNDRI).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-Rothman_2003-149] MDMA enters [monoaminergic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoaminergic) [neurons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuron) via the MATs and then, via poorly understood [mechanisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechanism_of_action), reverses the direction of these transporters to produce [efflux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efflux_pump) of the [monoamine neurotransmitters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_neurotransmitter) rather than the usual [reuptake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuptake).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Sulzer_2005-151][^cite_note-Reith_2020-152][^cite_note-Vaughan_2024-153][^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154] Induction of monoamine efflux by [amphetamines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine-type_stimulant) in general may involve [intracellular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intracellular) [Na<sup>+</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sodium_ion) and [Ca<sup>2+</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcium_ion) elevation and [PKC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protein_kinase_C) and [CaMKIIα](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CaMKII%CE%B1) activation.[^cite_note-Sulzer_2005-151][^cite_note-Reith_2020-152][^cite_note-Vaughan_2024-153] MDMA also acts on the [vesicular monoamine transporter 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vesicular_monoamine_transporter_2) (VMAT2) on [synaptic vesicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synaptic_vesicle) to increase the [cytosolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytosol) concentrations of the monoamine neurotransmitters available for efflux.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148] By inducing release and reuptake inhibition of [serotonin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin), [norepinephrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norepinephrine), and [dopamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dopamine), MDMA increases levels of these neurotransmitters in the [brain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain) and [periphery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peripheral_nervous_system) and thereby indirectly activates the [receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Receptor_\(biochemistry\)) of these neurotransmitters.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154] There are species differences in the balance of MAT activities of MDMA, with it showing greater influence on serotonin in rodents than in humans.[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154]

In addition to its actions as an SNDRA, MDMA directly interacts with a number of [monoamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monoamine_receptor) and other [receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Receptor_\(biochemistry\)).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-PDSPKiDatabase-135][^cite_note-BindingDB-136][^cite_note-Ray_2010-138][^cite_note-Jain_2025-137] With varying [potencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potency_\(pharmacology\)) ranging from high to low, it is a [partial agonist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partial_agonist) of several of the serotonin [5-HT<sub>1</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT1_receptor), including the serotonin [5-HT<sub>1A</sub>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT1A_receptor), [5-HT<sub>1B</sub>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT1B_receptor), and [5-HT<sub>1D</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT1D_receptor), and is a partial agonist of the serotonin [5-HT<sub>2</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2_receptor), including the serotonin [5-HT<sub>2A</sub>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2A_receptor), [5-HT<sub>2B</sub>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2B_receptor), and [5-HT<sub>2C</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-HT2C_receptor).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Jain_2025-137][^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Setola_2003-156][^cite_note-Nash_1994-157] The drug also interacts with [α<sub>2</sub>-adrenergic receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpha-2_adrenergic_receptor), with the [sigma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigma_receptor) [σ<sub>1</sub>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigma-1_receptor) and [σ<sub>2</sub> receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigma-2_receptor), with the [imidazoline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imidazoline_receptor) [I<sub>1</sub> receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I1_receptor), and with other [targets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological_target).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-PDSPKiDatabase-135][^cite_note-BindingDB-136][^cite_note-Jain_2025-137][^cite_note-Ray_2010-138] Along with the preceding receptor interactions, MDMA is a potent partial agonist of the rodent [trace amine-associated receptor 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trace_amine-associated_receptor_1) (TAAR1).[^cite_note-Gainetdinov_2018-144][^cite_note-Simmler_2016-145] Conversely however, due to species differences, it is far weaker in terms of potency as an agonist of the human TAAR1.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Gainetdinov_2018-144][^cite_note-Simmler_2016-145][^cite_note-Lewin_2011-158] The drug appears to act as a weak partial agonist of the human TAAR1 rather than as an [efficacious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intrinsic_activity) agonist.[^cite_note-Gainetdinov_2018-144][^cite_note-Simmler_2016-145] In relation to the preceding findings, MDMA has been said to be essentially inactive as a human TAAR1 agonist.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19] TAAR1 activation is thought to auto-inhibit and constrain the effects of amphetamines that possess TAAR1 agonism, for instance MDMA in rodents.[^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-Espinoza_2014-159][^cite_note-Kuropka_2023-160][^cite_note-Simmler_2013-139][^cite_note-Di_Cara_2011-161]

Elevation of serotonin, norepinephrine, and dopamine levels by MDMA is believed to mediate most of the drug's effects, including its entactogenic, stimulant, euphoriant, [hyperthermic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperthermia), and [sympathomimetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sympathomimetic) effects.[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148][^cite_note-Rein_2024-162][^cite_note-KamilarBritt_2015-72] The entactogenic effects of MDMA, including increased [sociability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociability), [empathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empathy), [feelings of closeness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emotional_intimacy), and [reduced aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiaggressive), are thought to be mainly due to induction of serotonin release.[^cite_note-KamilarBritt_2015-72][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20] The exact [serotonin receptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_receptor) responsible for MDMA's entactogenic effects are unclear, but may include the serotonin 5-HT<sub>1A</sub> receptor,[^cite_note-Esaki_2023-163] 5-HT<sub>1B</sub> receptor,[^cite_note-Heifets_2019-164] and 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor,[^cite_note-Pitts_2017-165] as well as 5-HT<sub>1A</sub> receptor-mediated [oxytocin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxytocin) release and consequent activation of the [oxytocin receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxytocin_receptor).[^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-KamilarBritt_2015-72][^cite_note-BlancoGandia_2015-166][^cite_note-Heifets_2024-167][^cite_note-Nichols_2022-147][^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154] The role of oxytocin in the effects of MDMA was demonstrated in people with [arginine vasopressin deficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arginine_vasopressin_deficiency) (central diabetes insipidus), who have [oxytocin deficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxytocin_deficiency) and in whom MDMA fails to elevate oxytocin levels and shows greatly blunted entactogenic effects.[^cite_note-VogtLiechti2025-126][^cite_note-AtilaRefardtChrist-Crain2026-168][^cite_note-AtilaHolzeMurugesu2023-169] Induction of dopamine release is thought to be importantly involved in the stimulant and euphoriant effects of MDMA,[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154][^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19][^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Kaur_2023-170] while induction of norepinephrine release and serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor stimulation are believed to mediate its sympathomimetic effects.[^cite_note-Fonseca_2021-117][^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148] Activation of serotonin 5-HT<sub>1B</sub> and 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptors is also thought to be involved in the stimulant and euphoriant effects of MDMA, while serotonin 5-HT<sub>2C</sub> receptor activation is thought to constrain these effects and limit MDMA's [reinforcing potential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_addiction).[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154][^cite_note-Bankson_2001-171][^cite_note-Baumann_2008-172][^cite_note-Bankson_2002-173][^cite_note-Cole_2003-174][^cite_note-Pomrenze_2025-175][^cite_note-Canal_2017-176] Serotonin 5-HT<sub>2B</sub> receptor signaling appears to be required for MDMA-induced serotonin release and effects.[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154][^cite_note-MartinezPrice_2002-177][^cite_note-Stove_2010-178][^cite_note-Doly_2008-179][^cite_note-Doly_2009-180][^cite_note-Belmer_2018-181] MDMA has been associated with a unique subjective "magic" or [euphoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphoria) that few or no other known entactogens are said to fully reproduce.[^cite_note-Baggott_2023-182][^cite_note-Baggott2024-183] The mechanisms underlying this property of MDMA are unknown, but it has been theorized to be due to a specific mixture and balance of pharmacological activities, including combined serotonin, norepinephrine, and dopamine release and direct serotonin receptor agonism.[^cite_note-Rothman_2002-184][^cite_note-Baggott_2023-182][^cite_note-Baggott2024-183][^cite_note-Luethi_2020-185]

MDMA is often said to have mild or weak [psychedelic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_drug) effects.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20][^cite_note-Schenk_2018-187] These effects are said to be [dose-dependent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dose_dependence), such that greater [hallucinogenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucinogen) effects are produced at higher doses.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Puxty_2017-188] The mild hallucinogenic effects of MDMA include [perceptual changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucination) like intensification of [visual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_perception), [auditory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auditory_perception), and [tactile perception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tactile_perception) (e.g., brightened colors), a state of [dissociation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissociation_\(psychology\)) with feelings of [depersonalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Depersonalization) and [derealization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derealization) (e.g., "oceanic boundlessness"), and [thinking disturbances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thought_disorder).[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Meyer_2013a-189][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Schenk_2018-187][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122][^cite_note-Liechti_2000b-190][^cite_note-Puxty_2017-188] Conversely, overt [hallucinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucination) do not occur, MDMA's hallucinogenic effects are described as "non-problematic" for users, and are said to be much less than those of [3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine) (MDA) or especially those of fully effective [serotonergic psychedelics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonergic_psychedelic) like [psilocybin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psilocybin).[^cite_note-Meyer_2013a-189][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20] The hallucinogenic effects of MDMA have been theorized to be mediated by serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor activation analogously to the case of classical psychedelics.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122][^cite_note-Puxty_2017-188][^cite_note-Simmler_2018-191][^cite_note-Meyer_2013a-189][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18] Accordingly, the serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor [antagonist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antagonist) ketanserin has been reported to reduce MDMA-induced perceptual changes in humans.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122][^cite_note-Puxty_2017-188] Conversely however, it failed to affect MDMA-induced feelings of dissociation and oceanic boundlessness.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Puxty_2017-188] In contrast, the serotonin reuptake inhibitor [citalopram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citalopram), which blocks MDMA-induced serotonin release, diminished all of the psychoactive and hallucinogenic effects of MDMA.[^cite_note-Liechti_2001-186][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Liechti_2000b-190][^cite_note-Liechti_2000-122] It has been noted that *N*-[methylation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylation) of psychedelic [phenethylamines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_phenethylamine), as in the [structural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_structure) difference between MDA and MDMA, has invariably greatly reduced or abolished their psychedelic activity.[^cite_note-Nichols_2018-192][^cite_note-Jacob_1994-193] Whereas MDA and psychedelics like psilocybin induce the [head-twitch response](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head-twitch_response) in rodents, a behavioral proxy of psychedelic effects, findings on MDMA and the head-twitch response are mixed and conflicting.[^cite_note-Halberstadt_2018-194][^cite_note-RosadoYuYang2026-195][^cite_note-Dunlap_2022-196][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120] In addition, whereas MDA fully substitutes for psychedelics like [LSD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LSD) and [DOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DOM_\(drug\)) in rodent [drug discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_discrimination) tests, MDMA does not do so, nor do psychedelics generally fully substitute for MDMA.[^cite_note-Nichols_2016-197][^cite_note-Halberstadt_2020-120][^cite_note-Nichols_1989-198][^cite_note-Nichols_1990-199]

Long-term repeated activation of serotonin 5-HT<sub>2B</sub> receptors by MDMA is thought to result in increased risk of organ complications such as [valvular heart disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valvular_heart_disease) (VHD) and [primary pulmonary hypertension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_pulmonary_hypertension) (PPH).[^cite_note-McIntyre_2023-200][^cite_note-Tagen_2023-201][^cite_note-Wso_2023-123][^cite_note-Rothman_2009-202][^cite_note-Rothman_2002-184][^cite_note-Rothman_2002a-203] MDMA has been associated with [serotonergic neurotoxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonergic_neurotoxicity).[^cite_note-Costa_2022-204][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20][^cite_note-Sprague_1998-205] This may be due to formation of toxic MDMA [metabolites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metabolite) and/or induction of [simultaneous serotonin and dopamine release](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine%E2%80%93dopamine_releasing_agent), with consequent uptake of dopamine into serotonergic neurons and breakdown into [toxic species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactive_oxygen_species).[^cite_note-Costa_2022-204][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20][^cite_note-Sprague_1998-205] Serotonin 5-HT<sub>2</sub> receptor agonists or serotonergic psychedelics may potentiate the neurotoxicity of MDMA.[^cite_note-GreenMechanElliott2003-206][^cite_note-GudelskyYamamotoNash1994-207][^cite_note-SchmidtBlackAbbate1990-208][^cite_note-Armstrong_2004-209]

MDMA is a [racemic mixture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racemic_mixture) of two [enantiomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enantiomers), (*S*)-MDMA and [(*R*)-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\(R\)-MDMA).[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18] (*S*)-MDMA is much more potent as an SNDRA *[in vitro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_vitro)* and in producing MDMA-like subjective effects in humans than (*R*)-MDMA.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Rothman_2006-150][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18][^cite_note-Anderson_1978-210] By contrast, (*R*)-MDMA acts as a lower-potency [serotonin–norepinephrine releasing agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine_releasing_agent) (SNRA) with weak or negligible effects on dopamine.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Rothman_2006-150][^cite_note-Acquas_2007-211] Relatedly, (*R*)-MDMA shows weak or negligible stimulant-like and [rewarding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reward_system) effects in animals.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Curry_2018-212] Both (*S*)-MDMA and (*R*)-MDMA produce entactogen-type effects in animals and humans.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18] In addition, both (*S*)-MDMA and (*R*)-MDMA are weak agonists of the serotonin 5-HT<sub>2</sub> receptors.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Kaur_2023-170][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18][^cite_note-Setola_2003-156][^cite_note-Nash_1994-157] (*R*)-MDMA is more potent and efficacious as a serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> and 5-HT<sub>2B</sub> receptor agonist than (*S*)-MDMA, whereas (*S*)-MDMA is somewhat more potent as an agonist of the serotonin 5-HT<sub>2C</sub> receptor.[^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Kaur_2023-170][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18] Due to it being a more potent serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub> receptor agonist than (*S*)-MDMA, (*R*)-MDMA has been hypothesized to have greater psychedelic effects than (*S*)-MDMA or racemic MDMA.[^cite_note-Bedi_2024-213][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18] However, this proved not to be the case in a direct clinical comparison of (*R*)-MDMA, (*S*)-MDMA, and racemic MDMA, with equivalent hallucinogen-like effects instead found between the three interventions.[^cite_note-Bedi_2024-213][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18]

MDMA produces MDA as a minor [active metabolite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_metabolite).[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111] [Peak levels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cmax_\(pharmacology\)) of MDA are about 5 to 10% of those of MDMA and [total exposure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area-under-the-curve_\(pharmacokinetics\)) to MDA is almost 10% of that of MDMA with [oral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_administration) MDMA administration.[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111][^cite_note-Tagen_2023-201] As a result, MDA may contribute to some extent to the effects of MDMA.[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111][^cite_note-Simmler_2018-191] MDA is an entactogen, stimulant, and weak psychedelic similarly to MDMA.[^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20] Like MDMA, it acts as a potent and well-balanced SNDRA and as a weak serotonin 5-HT<sub>2</sub> receptor agonist.[^cite_note-Rothman_2006-150][^cite_note-Setola_2003-156][^cite_note-Nash_1994-157] However, MDA shows much more potent and efficacious serotonin 5-HT<sub>2A</sub>, 5-HT<sub>2B</sub>, and 5-HT<sub>2C</sub> receptor agonism than MDMA.[^cite_note-Kaur_2023-170][^cite_note-Simmler_2018-191][^cite_note-Nash_1994-157][^cite_note-Setola_2003-156] Accordingly, MDA produces greater psychedelic effects than MDMA in humans[^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20] and might particularly contribute to the mild psychedelic-like effects of MDMA.[^cite_note-VogtLiechti2025-126][^cite_note-Simmler_2018-191] On the other hand, MDA may also be importantly involved in [toxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxicity) of MDMA, such as [cardiac valvulopathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_valvulopathy).[^cite_note-Luethi_2021-214][^cite_note-Tagen_2023-201][^cite_note-Setola_2003-156]

The [duration of action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duration_of_action) of MDMA (3–6 hours) is much shorter than its [elimination half-life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elimination_half-life) (8–9 hours) would imply.[^cite_note-VogtLiechti2025-126][^cite_note-Mead_2020-215] In relation to this, MDMA's duration and the offset of its effects appear to be determined more by [rapid acute tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tachyphylaxis) rather than by circulating drug concentrations.[^cite_note-VogtLiechti2025-126][^cite_note-Hysek_2012-42] Similar findings have been made for [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine) and [methamphetamine](11038318.md).[^cite_note-Ermer_2016-216][^cite_note-Cruickshank_2009-217][^cite_note-Abbas_2024-218][^cite_note-van_Gaalen_2019-219] One mechanism by which [tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_tolerance) to MDMA may occur is [internalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endocytosis) of the [serotonin transporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotonin_transporter) (SERT).[^cite_note-Bisagno_2021-220][^cite_note-Kivell_2010-221][^cite_note-Holley_2013-222][^cite_note-Underhill_2020-223][^cite_note-Underhill_2022-224] Although MDMA and serotonin are not significant TAAR1 agonists in humans, TAAR1 activation by MDMA may result in SERT internalization, for instance in rodents in whom MDMA is a potent TAAR1 agonist.[^cite_note-Underhill_2020-223][^cite_note-Underhill_2022-224][^cite_note-Kittler_2010-225][^cite_note-Gainetdinov_2018-144] It is thought that brain serotonin levels are depleted after MDMA administration but that levels typically return to normal within 24 to 48 hours.[^cite_note-Betzler_2017-8]

### Pharmacokinetics

#### Absorption

The MDMA [concentration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration) in the [bloodstream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloodstream) starts to rise after about 30 minutes,[^cite_note-233] and reaches its maximal [concentration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration) between 1.5 and 3 hours after [oral administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_administration).[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2000-234] It is then slowly [metabolized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metabolism) and [excreted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excretion), with levels of MDMA and its metabolites decreasing to half their peak concentration over the next several hours.[^cite_note-Farre_2000-235] The [duration of action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duration_of_action) of MDMA is about 3 to 6 hours.[^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20]

#### Distribution

The [plasma protein binding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plasma_protein_binding) of MDMA is unknown.[^cite_note-DrugBank-15]

#### Metabolism



Main [metabolic pathways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metabolic_pathway) of MDMA in humans.

[Metabolites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metabolite) of MDMA that have been identified in humans include [3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-Methylenedioxyamphetamine) (MDA), [4-hydroxy-3-methoxymethamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4-hydroxy-3-methoxymethamphetamine) (HMMA), [4-hydroxy-3-methoxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4-hydroxy-3-methoxyamphetamine) (HMA), [3,4-dihydroxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpha-Methyldopamine) (DHA) (also called alpha-methyldopamine (α-Me-DA)), [3,4-dhydroxymethamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-Dihydroxymethamphetamine) (HHMA), [3,4-methylenedioxyphenylacetone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDP2P) (MDP2P), and [3,4-methylenedioxy-N-hydroxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylenedioxyhydroxyamphetamine) (MDOH). The contributions of these metabolites to the psychoactive and [toxic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxic) effects of MDMA are an area of active research. 80% of MDMA is metabolised in the liver, and about 20% is excreted unchanged in the [urine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urine).[^cite_note-pmid22392347-16]

MDMA is known to be metabolized by two main [metabolic pathways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metabolic_pathway): (1) *O*-demethylenation followed by [catechol-*O*-methyltransferase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechol-O-methyl_transferase) (COMT)-catalyzed methylation or glucuronide/sulfate conjugation; and (2) *N*-dealkylation, deamination, and oxidation to the corresponding [benzoic acid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzoic_acid) derivatives conjugated with [glycine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glycine).[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2004-111] The metabolism may be primarily by [cytochrome P450](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytochrome_P450_oxidase) (CYP450) [enzymes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzyme) [CYP2D6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CYP2D6) and [CYP3A4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CYP3A4) and COMT. Complex, nonlinear [pharmacokinetics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmacokinetics) arise via autoinhibition of [CYP2D6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CYP2D6) and CYP2D8, resulting in [zeroth order kinetics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_equation) at higher doses. It is thought that this can result in sustained and higher [concentrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration) of MDMA if the user takes consecutive doses of the drug.[^cite_note-Kolbrich_2008-236]

#### Elimination

MDMA and metabolites are primarily excreted as conjugates, such as sulfates and glucuronides.[^cite_note-Shima_2007-237] MDMA is a [chiral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chirality_\(chemistry\)) compound and has been almost exclusively administered as a [racemate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racemic). However, the two enantiomers have been shown to exhibit different kinetics. The disposition of MDMA may also be stereoselective, with the S-enantiomer having a shorter elimination half-life and greater excretion than the R-enantiomer. Evidence suggests[^cite_note-Fallon_1999-238] that the area under the [blood plasma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_plasma) concentration versus time curve (AUC) was two to four times higher for the (*R*)-enantiomer than the (*S*)-enantiomer after a 40 mg oral dose in human volunteers. Likewise, the plasma half-life of [(*R*)-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\(R\)-MDMA) was significantly longer than that of the (*S*)-enantiomer (5.8 ± 2.2 hours vs 3.6 ± 0.9 hours).[^cite_note-Toxnet_MDMA_after-effects-63] However, because MDMA excretion and metabolism have nonlinear kinetics,[^cite_note-Mueller_2008-239] the half-lives would be higher at more typical doses (100 mg is sometimes considered a typical dose).[^cite_note-de_la_Torre_2000-234]

## Chemistry



MDMA is a [racemic mixture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racemic_mixture) and exists as two [enantiomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enantiomers): [(*R*)-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\(R\)-MDMA) (top) and (*S*)-MDMA (bottom). Depicted in this figure are their skeletal structures.

A powdered salt of MDMA

A powdered salt of MDMA

Reactors used in synthesis

Reactors used to synthesize MDMA on an industrial scale in a [clandestine chemical factory](610329.md) in Cikande, [Indonesia](14579.md)

MDMA, also known as 3,4-methylenedioxy-*N*-methylamphetamine or as 1-(1,3-benzodioxol-5-yl)-*N*-methylpropan-2-amine, is a [chemical compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_compound) of the [phenethylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_phenethylamine), [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_amphetamine), and [methylenedioxyphenethylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_methylenedioxyphenethylamine) (MDxx) families.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240] It is a [derivative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_derivative) of various related compounds including [phenethylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phenethylamine), [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine), [methamphetamine](11038318.md), [homopiperonylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homopiperonylamine) (MDPEA), and [3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine) (MDA).[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240] MDMA is a [racemic mixture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racemic_mixture) of two [enantiomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enantiomer), [(*R*)-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\(R\)-MDMA) and (*S*)-MDMA.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-Pitts_2018-155][^cite_note-Straumann_2024-18]

### Properties

The chemical properties of MDMA have been described.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-EU2015-10] As a [free base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_base), MDMA is a colorless oil insoluble in water.[^cite_note-EU2015-10] The most common [salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_\(chemistry\)) of MDMA is the [hydrochloride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrochloride) salt.[^cite_note-EU2015-10] Pure MDMA hydrochloride is water-soluble and appears as a white or off-white powder or crystal.[^cite_note-EU2015-10]

### Synthesis

The [chemical synthesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_synthesis) of MDMA has been described.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-Dunlap_2018-19] There are numerous methods available to synthesize MDMA via different intermediates.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] The original MDMA synthesis described in Merck's patent involves brominating [safrole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safrole) to 1-(3,4-methylenedioxyphenyl)-2-bromopropane and then reacting this adduct with methylamine.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] Most illicit MDMA is synthesized using [MDP2P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDP2P) (3,4-methylenedioxyphenyl-2-propanone) as a precursor. MDP2P in turn is generally synthesized from [piperonal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piperonal), [safrole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safrole) or [isosafrole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isosafrole).[^cite_note-World_Drug_Report_2014-247] One method is to [isomerize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isomerization) safrole to isosafrole in the presence of a strong base, and then oxidize [isosafrole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isosafrole) to MDP2P. Another method uses the [Wacker process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wacker_process) to oxidize safrole directly to the MDP2P intermediate with a [palladium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palladium) catalyst. Once the MDP2P intermediate has been prepared, a [reductive amination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reductive_amination) leads to [racemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racemic) MDMA (an equal parts mixture of [(*R*)-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\(R\)-MDMA) and (*S*)-MDMA). Relatively small quantities of essential oil are required to make large amounts of MDMA. The essential oil of *[Ocotea cymbarum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocotea_cymbarum)*, for example, typically contains between 80 and 94% safrole. This allows 500 mL of the oil to produce between 150 and 340 grams of MDMA.[^cite_note-248]
