### Detection in body fluids

MDMA and MDA may be quantitated in blood, plasma or urine to monitor for use, confirm a diagnosis of poisoning, or assist in the forensic investigation of a traffic or other criminal violation or a sudden death. Some drug abuse screening programs rely on hair, saliva, or sweat as specimens. Most commercial amphetamine immunoassay screening tests cross-react significantly with MDMA or its major metabolites, but chromatographic techniques can easily distinguish and separately measure each of these substances. The concentrations of MDA in the blood or urine of a person who has taken only MDMA are, in general, less than 10% those of the parent drug.[^cite_note-Kolbrich_2008-236][^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250]

### Analogues

See also: [Substituted methylenedioxyphenethylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substituted_methylenedioxyphenethylamine)

[Analogues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Structural_analog) of MDMA include [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine), [methamphetamine](11038318.md), [homopiperonylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homopiperonylamine) (MDPEA), [homarylamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homarylamine) (MDMPEA), [lophophine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lophophine) (MMDPEA), [MDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDA_\(drug\)), [MMDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MMDA_\(drug\)), [MMDA-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MMDA-2_\(drug\)), [DMMDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DMMDA_\(drug\)), [DMMDA-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DMMDA-2_\(drug\)), [MDEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDEA_\(drug\)), [MDOH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDOH_\(drug\)), [MDMOH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDMOH_\(drug\)) (FLEA), [*N*-*t*-BOC-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N-t-BOC-MDMA), [methylone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylone) (MDMC), [MBDB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBDB), [5-MAPB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-MAPB), [6-MAPB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6-MAPB), [5-MAPBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5-MAPBT), [6-MAPBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6-MAPBT), [SDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SDMA_\(drug\)), [ODMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ODMA_\(drug\)), [SeDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeDMA_\(drug\)), [TDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TDMA_\(drug\)), [MDAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDAI_\(drug\)), and [MDAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDAT_\(drug\)), among many others.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-Oeri_2021-20] [Deuterated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deuterated_drug) analogues of MDMA such as [d2-MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D2-MDMA) have been described as well.[^cite_note-FukutoKumagaiCho1991-251][^cite_note-LinLiuYin2004-252][^cite_note-RombergNtamackBlacik2011-253][^cite_note-BerquistLeth-PetersenKristensen2020a-254][^cite_note-BerquistLeth-PetersenKristensen2020b-255]

## History

### Early research and use

Merck MDMA synthesis patent

Merck patent for synthesizing methylhydrastinine from MDMA

German patents for MDMA synthesis and the subsequent methylhydrastinine synthesis filed by Merck on 24 December 1912 and issued in 1914

MDMA was first [synthesized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_synthesis) and [patented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patent) in 1912 by [Merck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merck_KGaA) chemist [Anton Köllisch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_K%C3%B6llisch).[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-BernschneiderReif_2006-257] At the time, Merck was interested in developing substances that stopped abnormal bleeding. Merck wanted to avoid an existing patent held by [Bayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayer) for one such compound: [hydrastinine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrastinine). Köllisch developed a preparation of a hydrastinine [analogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_analogue), methylhydrastinine, at the request of fellow lab members, Walther Beckh and Otto Wolfes. MDMA (called methylsafrylamin, safrylmethylamin or N-Methyl-a-Methylhomopiperonylamin in Merck laboratory reports) was an [intermediate compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reaction_intermediate) in the synthesis of methylhydrastinine. Merck was not interested in MDMA itself at the time.[^cite_note-BernschneiderReif_2006-257] On 24 December 1912, Merck filed two patent applications that described the synthesis and some chemical properties of MDMA[^cite_note-Darmstadt_1914-258] and its subsequent conversion to methylhydrastinine.[^cite_note-Darmstadt_1914a-259] Merck records indicate its researchers returned to the compound sporadically. A 1920 Merck patent describes a chemical modification to MDMA.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-Shulgin_1990-260]

MDMA's [analogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Structural_analog) [3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3,4-methylenedioxyamphetamine) (MDA) was first synthesized in 1910 as a [derivative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_derivative) of [adrenaline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrenaline).[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256] [Gordon A. Alles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon_A._Alles), the discoverer of the [psychoactive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychoactive_drug) effects of [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine), also discovered the psychoactive effects of MDA in 1930 in a [self-experiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-experiment) in which he administered a high dose (126 mg) to himself.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-Alles_1959a-261][^cite_note-Alles_1959b-262] However, he did not subsequently describe these effects until 1959.[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-Alles_1959a-261][^cite_note-Alles_1959b-262] MDA was later tested as an [appetite suppressant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appetite_suppressant) by [Smith, Kline & French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smith,_Kline_&_French) and for other uses by other groups in the 1950s.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256] In relation to the preceding, the psychoactive effects of MDA were discovered well before those of MDMA.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263]

In 1927, Max Oberlin studied the pharmacology of MDMA while searching for substances with effects similar to [adrenaline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrenaline) or [ephedrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephedrine), the latter being structurally similar to MDMA. Compared to ephedrine, Oberlin observed that it had similar effects on [vascular smooth muscle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vascular_smooth_muscle) tissue, stronger effects at the uterus, and no "local effect at the eye". MDMA was also found to have effects on [blood sugar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_sugar) levels comparable to high doses of ephedrine. Oberlin concluded that the effects of MDMA were not limited to the [sympathetic nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sympathetic_nervous_system). Research was stopped "particularly due to a strong price increase of safrylmethylamine", which was still used as an intermediate in methylhydrastinine synthesis. Albert van Schoor performed simple toxicological tests with the drug in 1952, most likely while researching new stimulants or circulatory medications. After pharmacological studies, research on MDMA was not continued. In 1959, Wolfgang Fruhstorfer synthesized MDMA for pharmacological testing while researching stimulants. It is unclear if Fruhstorfer investigated the effects of MDMA in humans.[^cite_note-BernschneiderReif_2006-257]

Outside of Merck, other researchers began to investigate MDMA. In 1953 and 1954, the [United States Army](32087.md) commissioned a study of [toxicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxicity) and behavioral effects in animals injected with [mescaline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mescaline) and several analogues, including MDMA. Conducted at the [University of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Michigan) in [Ann Arbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Arbor), these investigations were declassified in October 1969 and published in 1973.[^cite_note-Hardman_1973-264][^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263] A 1960 Polish paper by Biniecki and Krajewski describing the synthesis of MDMA as an intermediate was the first published scientific paper on the substance.[^cite_note-BernschneiderReif_2006-257][^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-265]

The first administration of MDMA to humans is unknown.[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154] MDA appeared as a [recreational drug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recreational_drug) in the mid-1960s.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256] MDMA may have been in non-medical use in the western United States in 1968.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-Siegel_1986-266] An August 1970 report at a meeting of crime laboratory chemists indicates MDMA was being used recreationally in the Chicago area by 1970.[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154][^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-267] MDMA likely emerged as a substitute for MDA,[^cite_note-Foderaro_1988-268] a drug at the time popular among users of psychedelics[^cite_note-Professor_X-269] which was made a [Schedule 1 controlled substance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Schedule_I_controlled_substances_\(U.S.\)) in the United States in 1970.[^cite_note-Beck_1987-270][^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271]

### Shulgin's research



Alexander and Ann Shulgin in December 2011

American chemist and [psychopharmacologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychopharmacologist) [Alexander Shulgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Shulgin) reported he synthesized MDMA in 1965 while researching methylenedioxy compounds at [Dow Chemical Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dow_Chemical_Company), but did not test the psychoactivity of the compound at this time. Around 1970, Shulgin sent instructions for N-methylated MDA (MDMA) synthesis to the founder of a Los Angeles chemical company who had requested them. This individual later provided these instructions to a client in the Midwest. Shulgin may have suspected he played a role in the emergence of MDMA in Chicago.[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263]

Shulgin first heard of the psychoactive effects of N-methylated MDA around 1975 from a young student who reported "amphetamine-like content".[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263] Around 30 May 1976, Shulgin again heard about the effects of N-methylated MDA,[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263] this time from a graduate student in a medicinal chemistry group he advised at [San Francisco State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_State_University)[^cite_note-Professor_X-269][^cite_note-272] who directed him to the University of Michigan study.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240] She and two close friends had consumed 100 mg of MDMA and reported positive emotional experiences.[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263] Following the self-trials of a colleague at the [University of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_San_Francisco), Shulgin synthesized MDMA and tried it himself in September and October 1976.[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-Professor_X-269] Shulgin and [David E. Nichols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_E._Nichols) first reported on MDMA and its psychoactive effects in humans in a presentation at a conference in Bethesda, Maryland in December 1976.[^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-ShulginNichols1978-273] This conference paper was subsequently formally published in 1978.[^cite_note-Passie_2023-256][^cite_note-Benzenhofer_2010-263][^cite_note-ShulginNichols1978-273] They described MDMA as inducing "an easily controlled altered state of consciousness with emotional and sensual overtones" comparable "to marijuana, to [psilocybin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psilocybin) devoid of the hallucinatory component, or to low levels of MDA".[^cite_note-ShulginNichols1978-273]

While not finding his own experiences with MDMA particularly powerful,[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-Dr._Ecstasy-274] Shulgin was impressed with the drug's disinhibiting effects and thought it could be useful in therapy.[^cite_note-Dr._Ecstasy-274] Believing MDMA allowed users to strip away habits and perceive the world clearly, Shulgin called the drug *window*.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240][^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275] Shulgin occasionally used MDMA for relaxation, referring to it as "my low-calorie martini", and gave the drug to friends, researchers, and others who he thought could benefit from it.[^cite_note-PiHKAL-240] One such person was [Leo Zeff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Zeff), a psychotherapist who had been known to use psychedelic substances in his practice. When he tried the drug in 1977, Zeff was impressed with the effects of MDMA and came out of his semi-retirement to promote its use in therapy. Over the following years, Zeff traveled around the United States and occasionally to Europe, eventually training an estimated four thousand psychotherapists in the therapeutic use of MDMA.[^cite_note-Dr._Ecstasy-274][^cite_note-276] Zeff named the drug *Adam*, believing it put users in a state of primordial innocence.[^cite_note-Professor_X-269]

Psychotherapists who used MDMA believed the drug eliminated the typical fear response and increased communication. Sessions were usually held in the home of the patient or the therapist. The role of the therapist was minimized in favor of patient self-discovery accompanied by MDMA induced feelings of empathy. Depression, substance use disorders, relationship problems, premenstrual syndrome, and autism were among several psychiatric disorders MDMA assisted therapy was reported to treat.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271] According to psychiatrist George Greer, therapists who used MDMA in their practice were impressed by the results. Anecdotally, MDMA was said to greatly accelerate therapy.[^cite_note-Dr._Ecstasy-274] According to [David Nutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Nutt), MDMA was widely used in the western US in couples counseling, and was called *empathy*. Only later was the term *ecstasy* used for it, coinciding with rising opposition to its use.[^cite_note-Nutt-277][^cite_note-pmid10450215-278]

### Rising recreational use

In the late 1970s and early 1980s, "Adam" spread through personal networks of psychotherapists, psychiatrists, users of psychedelics, and [yuppies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuppies). Hoping MDMA could avoid criminalization like LSD and mescaline, psychotherapists and experimenters attempted to limit the spread of MDMA and information about it while conducting informal research.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271][^cite_note-Eisner-279] Early MDMA distributors were deterred from large scale operations by the threat of possible legislation.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] Between the 1970s and the mid-1980s, this network of MDMA users consumed an estimated 500,000 doses.[^cite_note-Meyer_2013-24][^cite_note-isbn0803936788-281]

A small recreational market for MDMA developed by the late 1970s,[^cite_note-Collin_2010-282] consuming perhaps 10,000 doses in 1976.[^cite_note-Beck_1987-270] By the early 1980s MDMA was being used in Boston and New York City nightclubs such as [Studio 54](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_54) and [Paradise Garage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradise_Garage).[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] Into the early 1980s, as the recreational market slowly expanded, production of MDMA was dominated by a small group of therapeutically minded [Boston](24437894.md) chemists. Having commenced production in 1976, this "Boston Group" did not keep up with growing demand and shortages frequently occurred.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280]

Perceiving a business opportunity, [Michael Clegg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Clegg_\(drug_distributor\)), the Southwest distributor for the Boston Group, started his own "Texas Group" backed financially by Texas friends.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280][^cite_note-285] He started by hosting private parties and offering MDMa to his guests for free.[^cite_note-286] In 1981,[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] Clegg had coined "Ecstasy" as a slang term for MDMA to increase its marketability.[^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275][^cite_note-Eisner-279] Starting in 1983,[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] the Texas Group mass-produced MDMA in a Texas lab[^cite_note-Eisner-279] or imported it from California[^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275] and marketed tablets using pyramid sales structures and toll-free numbers.[^cite_note-isbn0803936788-281] MDMA could be purchased via credit card and taxes were paid on sales.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] Under the brand name "Sassyfras", MDMA tablets were sold in brown bottles.[^cite_note-Eisner-279] The Texas Group advertised "Ecstasy parties" at bars and discos, describing MDMA as a "fun drug" and "good to dance to".[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] MDMA was openly distributed in [Austin](1998.md) and [Dallas–Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth) area bars and nightclubs, becoming popular with yuppies, college students, and gays.[^cite_note-Foderaro_1988-268][^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280][^cite_note-isbn0803936788-281]

Recreational use also increased after several cocaine dealers switched to distributing MDMA following experiences with the drug.[^cite_note-isbn0803936788-281] A California laboratory that analyzed confidentially submitted drug samples first detected MDMA in 1975. Over the following years the number of MDMA samples increased, eventually exceeding the number of MDA samples in the early 1980s.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] By the mid-1980s, MDMA use had spread to colleges around the United States.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280]<sup>: 33 </sup>

### Media attention and scheduling

#### United States



27 July 1984 Federal Register notice of the proposed MDMA scheduling

In an early media report on MDMA published in 1982, a [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md) (DEA) spokesman stated the agency would ban the drug if enough evidence for abuse could be found.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] By mid-1984, MDMA use was becoming more noticed. Bill Mandel reported on "Adam" in a 10 June [San Francisco Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Chronicle) article, but misidentified the drug as [methyloxymethylenedioxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MMDA_\(drug\)) (MMDA). In the next month, the World Health Organization identified MDMA as the only substance out of twenty phenethylamines to be seized a significant number of times.[^cite_note-Eisner-279]

After a year of planning and data collection, MDMA was proposed for [scheduling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Substances_Act) by the DEA on 27 July 1984, with a request for comments and objections.[^cite_note-Eisner-279][^cite_note-289] The DEA was surprised when a number of psychiatrists, psychotherapists, and researchers objected to the proposed scheduling and requested a hearing.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271] In a [Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek) article published the next year, a DEA pharmacologist stated that the agency had been unaware of its use among psychiatrists.[^cite_note-290] An initial hearing was held on 1 February 1985 at the DEA offices in Washington, D.C., with administrative law judge Francis L. Young presiding.[^cite_note-Eisner-279] It was decided there to hold three more hearings that year: Los Angeles on 10 June, Kansas City, Missouri on 10–11 July, and Washington, D.C., on 8–11 October.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271][^cite_note-Eisner-279]

Sensational media attention was given to the proposed criminalization and the reaction of MDMA proponents, effectively advertising the drug.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271] In response to the proposed scheduling, the Texas Group increased production from 1985 estimates of 30,000 tablets a month to as many as 8,000 per day, potentially making two million ecstasy tablets in the months before MDMA was made illegal.[^cite_note-comprehensive-291] By some estimates the Texas Group distributed 500,000 tablets per month in Dallas alone.[^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275] According to one participant in an [ethnographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnographic) study, the Texas Group produced more MDMA in eighteen months than all other distribution networks combined across their entire histories.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] By May 1985, MDMA use was widespread in California, Texas, southern Florida, and the northeastern United States.[^cite_note-Siegel_1986-266][^cite_note-New_York_Times-292] According to the DEA there was evidence of use in twenty-eight states[^cite_note-293] and Canada.[^cite_note-Siegel_1986-266] Urged by Senator [Lloyd Bentsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Bentsen), the DEA announced an [emergency Schedule I classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Crime_Control_Act_of_1984) of MDMA on 31 May 1985. The agency cited increased distribution in Texas, escalating street use, and new evidence of MDA (an analog of MDMA) neurotoxicity as reasons for the emergency measure.[^cite_note-New_York_Times-292][^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] The ban took effect one month later on 1 July 1985[^cite_note-comprehensive-291] in the midst of [Nancy Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Reagan)'s "[Just Say No](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_Say_No)" campaign.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297]

As a result of several expert witnesses testifying that MDMA had an accepted medical usage, the administrative law judge presiding over the hearings recommended that MDMA be classified as a [Schedule III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Substances_Act#Schedule_III_controlled_substances) substance. Despite this, DEA administrator [John C. Lawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_C._Lawn) overruled and classified the drug as Schedule I.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271][^cite_note-Harpers-298] Harvard psychiatrist [Lester Grinspoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lester_Grinspoon) then sued the DEA, claiming that the DEA had ignored the medical uses of MDMA, and the federal court sided with Grinspoon, calling Lawn's argument "strained" and "unpersuasive", and vacated MDMA's Schedule I status.[^cite_note-299] Despite this, less than a month later Lawn reviewed the evidence and reclassified MDMA as Schedule I again, claiming that the expert testimony of several psychiatrists claiming over 200 cases where MDMA had been used in a therapeutic context with positive results could be dismissed because they were not published in medical journals.[^cite_note-Pentney_2001-271] In 2017, the FDA [granted](#Research) [breakthrough therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakthrough_therapy) designation for its use with psychotherapy for PTSD. However, this designation has been questioned and problematized.[^cite_note-Halvorsen_1689–1690-300]

#### United Nations

While engaged in scheduling debates in the United States, the DEA also pushed for international scheduling.[^cite_note-comprehensive-291] In 1985, the [World Health Organization](33583.md)'s Expert Committee on Drug Dependence recommended that MDMA be placed in Schedule I of the 1971 United Nations [Convention on Psychotropic Substances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_Psychotropic_Substances). The committee made this recommendation on the basis of the pharmacological similarity of MDMA to previously scheduled drugs, reports of illicit trafficking in Canada, drug seizures in the United States, and lack of well-defined therapeutic use. While intrigued by reports of psychotherapeutic uses for the drug, the committee viewed the studies as lacking appropriate methodological design and encouraged further research. Committee chairman [Paul Grof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Grof) dissented, believing international control was not warranted at the time and a recommendation should await further therapeutic data.[^cite_note-301] The [Commission on Narcotic Drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_on_Narcotic_Drugs) added MDMA to Schedule I of the convention on 11 February 1986.[^cite_note-302]

### Post-scheduling



A 1995 Vibe Tribe rave in [Erskineville, New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erskineville,_New_South_Wales), Australia being broken up by police. MDMA use spread globally along with rave culture.

A 2000 [United States Air Force](32090.md) video dramatizing the dangers of MDMA misuse

The use of MDMA in Texas clubs declined rapidly after criminalization, but by 1991, the drug became popular among young middle-class whites and in nightclubs.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280] In 1985, MDMA use became associated with [acid house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acid_house) on the Spanish island of [Ibiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibiza).[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280]<sup>: 50 </sup>[^cite_note-303] Thereafter, in the late 1980s, the drug spread alongside [rave culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rave_culture) to the United Kingdom and then to other European and American cities.[^cite_note-Beck_&_Rosenbaum-280]<sup>: 50 </sup> Illicit MDMA use became increasingly widespread among young adults in universities and later, in high schools. Since the mid-1990s, MDMA has become the most widely used amphetamine-type drug by college students and teenagers.[^cite_note-Goldfrank_2011-304]<sup>: 1080 </sup> MDMA became one of the four most widely used illicit drugs in the US, along with [cocaine](7701.md), [heroin](14034.md), and [cannabis](1481886.md).[^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275] According to some estimates as of 2004, only marijuana attracts more first time users in the United States.[^cite_note-Jennings_2004-275]

After MDMA was criminalized, most medical use stopped, although some therapists continued to prescribe the drug illegally. Later, Charles Grob initiated an ascending-dose safety study in healthy volunteers. Subsequent FDA-approved MDMA studies in humans have taken place in the United States in Detroit ([Wayne State University](302260.md)), Chicago ([University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago)), San Francisco (UCSF and [California Pacific Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Pacific_Medical_Center)), [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore) ([NIDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_on_Drug_Abuse)–[NIH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NIH) Intramural Program), and [South Carolina](27956.md). Studies have also been conducted in Switzerland (University Hospital of Psychiatry, [Zürich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z%C3%BCrich)), the Netherlands ([Maastricht University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maastricht_University)), and Spain ([Universitat Autònoma de Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universitat_Aut%C3%B2noma_de_Barcelona)).[^cite_note-305]

"Molly", short for 'molecule', was recognized as a slang term for crystalline or powder MDMA in the 2000s.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307]

In 2010, the BBC reported that use of MDMA had decreased in the UK in previous years. This may be due to increased seizures during use and decreased production of the precursor chemicals used to manufacture MDMA. Unwitting substitution with other drugs, such as [mephedrone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mephedrone) and [methamphetamine](11038318.md),[^cite_note-308] as well as legal alternatives to MDMA, such as [BZP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzylpiperazine), [MDPV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylenedioxypyrovalerone), and [methylone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methylone), are also thought to have contributed to its decrease in popularity.[^cite_note-309]

In 2017, it was found that some pills being sold as MDMA contained [pentylone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentylone), which can cause very unpleasant agitation and paranoia.[^cite_note-310]

According to [David Nutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Nutt), when [safrole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safrole) was restricted by the United Nations in order to reduce the supply of MDMA, producers in China began using [anethole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anethole) instead, but this gives [para-methoxyamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Para-methoxyamphetamine) (PMA, also known as "Dr Death"), which is much more toxic than MDMA and can cause overheating, muscle spasms, seizures, unconsciousness, and death. People wanting MDMA are sometimes sold PMA instead.[^cite_note-Nutt-277]

In 2025, the BBC reported on a study of 650 survivors from the [Nova music festival massacre](75007485.md). Two-thirds were under the influence of recreational drugs (MDMA, LSD, marijuana or psilocybin) when [Hamas](13913.md) attacked the festival on [October 7, 2023](74998317.md). MDMA appeared to have a protective effect against later problems with sleeping and emotional distress.[^cite_note-Williamson_2025-311][^cite_note-Burns_2025-312]

## Society and culture

| [Cannabis](1481886.md) | 192.15 | 165.76 | 234.06 |

### Legal status

MDMA is legally controlled in most of the world under the UN [Convention on Psychotropic Substances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_Psychotropic_Substances) and other international agreements, although exceptions exist for research and limited medical use. In general, the unlicensed use, sale or manufacture of MDMA are all criminal offences.

#### Australia

In Australia, MDMA was rescheduled on 1 July 2023 as a schedule 8 substance (available on prescription) when used in the treatment of PTSD, while remaining a schedule 9 substance (prohibited) for all other uses. For the treatment of PTSD, MDMA can only be prescribed by psychiatrists with specific training and authorisation.[^cite_note-314] In 1986, MDMA was declared an illegal substance because of its allegedly harmful effects and potential for misuse.[^cite_note-315] Any non-authorised sale, use or manufacture is strictly prohibited by law. Permits for research uses on humans must be approved by a recognized [ethics committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Health_and_Medical_Research_Council) on human research.

In [Western Australia](33613.md) under the Misuse of Drugs Act 1981 4.0g of MDMA is the amount required determining a court of trial, 2.0g is considered a presumption with intent to sell or supply and 28.0g is considered trafficking under Australian law.[^cite_note-316]

The [Australian Capital Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Capital_Territory) passed legislation to decriminalise the possession of small amounts of MDMA, which took effect in October 2023.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-Roy_2023-318]

#### Canada

In Canada, MDMA is listed as a [Schedule 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Drugs_and_Substances_Act#Schedule_I)[^cite_note-CDSA_Schedule_I:_Amphetamines-319] as it is an analogue of amphetamine.[^cite_note-Definitions_and_Interpretations-320] The [Controlled Drugs and Substances Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Drugs_and_Substances_Act) was updated as a result of the [Safe Streets and Communities Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_Streets_and_Communities_Act) changing amphetamines from [Schedule III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Drugs_and_Substances_Act#Schedule_III) to Schedule I in March 2012. In 2022, the federal government granted [British Columbia](3392.md) a 3-year exemption, legalizing the possession of up to 2.5 grams (0.088 oz) of MDMA in the province from February 2023 until February 2026.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322]

#### Finland

Scheduled in the "government decree on substances, preparations and plants considered to be narcotic drugs".[^cite_note-323] Ecstasy is considered a very dangerous illegal drug.[^cite_note-324]

#### Netherlands

In 2024, a Dutch state commission issued a report advocating for MDMA to be made available to patients with PTSD.[^cite_note-325]

In June 2011, the Expert Committee on the List (Expertcommissie Lijstensystematiek Opiumwet) issued a report which discussed the evidence for harm and the legal status of MDMA, arguing in favor of maintaining it on List I.[^cite_note-Hennig_2014-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

#### United Kingdom

In the United Kingdom, MDMA was made illegal in 1977 by a modification order to the existing [Misuse of Drugs Act 1971](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misuse_of_Drugs_Act_1971). Although MDMA was not named explicitly in this legislation, the order extended the definition of Class A drugs to include various ring-substituted phenethylamines.[^cite_note-Drugs_2.0-329][^cite_note-UK_legality-330] The drug is therefore illegal to sell, buy, or possess without a licence in the UK. Penalties include a maximum of seven years and/or unlimited fine for possession; life and/or unlimited fine for production or trafficking.

Some researchers such as [David Nutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Nutt) have criticized the scheduling of MDMA, which he determined to be a relatively harmless drug.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] An editorial he wrote in the *[Journal of Psychopharmacology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journal_of_Psychopharmacology)*, where he compared the risk of harm for [horse riding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horse_Riding) (1 adverse event in 350) to that of ecstasy (1 in 10,000) resulted in his dismissal, leading to the resignation of several of his colleagues from the [ACMD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advisory_Council_on_the_Misuse_of_Drugs).[^cite_note-Johnson_2009-333]

#### United States

In the United States, MDMA is listed in [Schedule I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Substances_Act#Schedule_I_controlled_substances) of the [Controlled Substances Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_Substances_Act).[^cite_note-334] In a 2011 federal court hearing, the [American Civil Liberties Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_Liberties_Union) successfully argued that the sentencing guideline for MDMA/ecstasy is based on outdated science, leading to excessive prison sentences.[^cite_note-335] Other courts have upheld the sentencing guidelines. The [United States District Court for the Eastern District of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Tennessee) explained its ruling by noting that "an individual federal district court judge simply cannot marshal resources akin to those available to the Commission for tackling the manifold issues involved with determining a proper drug equivalency."[^cite_note-Hennig_2014-326]

### Usage statistics



UNODC map showing the use of ecstasy by country in 2014 for the global population aged 15–64

According to the [United Nations Office on Drugs and Crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Office_on_Drugs_and_Crime) World Drug Report, approximately 21 million people worldwide use ecstasy-type substances, including MDMA. This accounts for roughly 0.4% of the global population aged 15 to 64.[^cite_note-336] In the United States, according to the [Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substance_Abuse_and_Mental_Health_Services_Administration) 2021 National Survey on Drug Use and Health, among people aged 12 or older, 0.8% (2.2 million people) reported the use of ecstasy in the past year.[^cite_note-337]

Usage is much higher among attendees of nightclubs and dance festivals and among people aged in their late 20s.[^cite_note-338]

### Economics

#### Europe

In 2008, the [European Monitoring Centre for Drugs and Drug Addiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Monitoring_Centre_for_Drugs_and_Drug_Addiction) noted that although there were some reports of tablets being sold for as little as €1, most countries in Europe then reported typical retail prices in the range of €3 to €9 per tablet, typically containing 25–65 mg of MDMA.[^cite_note-339] By 2014 the EMCDDA reported that the range was more usually between €5 and €10 per tablet, typically containing 57–102 mg of MDMA, although MDMA in powder form was becoming more common.[^cite_note-340]

#### North America

The [United Nations Office on Drugs and Crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Office_on_Drugs_and_Crime) stated in its 2014 World Drug Report that US ecstasy retail prices range from US\$1 to \$70 per pill, or from \$15,000 to \$32,000 per kilogram.[^cite_note-341] A new research area named Drug Intelligence aims to automatically monitor distribution networks based on image processing and machine learning techniques, in which an Ecstasy pill picture is analyzed to detect correlations among different production batches.[^cite_note-342] These novel techniques allow police scientists to facilitate the monitoring of illicit distribution networks.

As of October 2015, most of the MDMA in the United States is produced in British Columbia, Canada and imported by Canada-based Asian [transnational criminal organizations](5841680.md).[^cite_note-DEA_2015_assessment-58] The market for MDMA in the United States is relatively small compared to [methamphetamine](11038318.md), [cocaine](7701.md), and [heroin](14034.md).[^cite_note-DEA_2015_assessment-58] In the United States, about 0.9 million people used ecstasy in 2010.[^cite_note-Drugs2014-26]

#### Australia

MDMA is particularly expensive in Australia, costing [A\$](81215.md)15–A\$30 per tablet. In terms of purity data for Australian MDMA, the average is around 34%, ranging from less than 1% to about 85%. The majority of tablets contain 70–85 mg of MDMA. Most MDMA enters Australia from the Netherlands, the UK, Asia, and the US.[^cite_note-343]

#### Corporate logos on pills

A number of ecstasy manufacturers brand their pills with a [logo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logo), often that of an unrelated corporation.[^cite_note-344] Some pills depict logos of products or media popular with children, such as [Shaun the Sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaun_the_Sheep).[^cite_note-345]

### In popular culture

#### Film

MDMA and ecstasy have featured in a number of films, including *[Trainspotting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trainspotting_\(film\))*, *[Clubbed to Death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clubbed_to_Death_\(film\))*, *[Nowhere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nowhere_\(1997_film\))*, *[Human Traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Traffic)*, *[Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_\(1999_film\))*, *[Groove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groove_\(film\))*, *[24 Hour Party People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24_Hour_Party_People)*, *[Bad Boys II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Boys_II)*, *[Party Monster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_Monster_\(film\))*, *[Layer Cake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Layer_Cake_\(film\))*, *[Rolling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_\(film\))*, *[Enter The Void](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enter_The_Void)*, *[Irvine Welsh's Ecstasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvine_Welsh's_Ecstasy)*, *[Spring Breakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Breakers)*, *[MDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDMA_\(film\))* and *[Beats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beats_\(2019_British_film\))*.[^cite_note-346]

#### Music

MDMA and ecstasy have featured in a number of songs, including [Miley Cyrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miley_Cyrus)'s "[We Can't Stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Can't_Stop)", [Jay-Z](162870.md) and [Alicia Keys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alicia_Keys)' "[Empire State of Mind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_State_of_Mind)", [Fujiya & Miyagi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujiya_&_Miyagi)'s "Seratonin Rushes", [Soulwax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soulwax)'s "[E Talking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E_Talking)", [Nonpoint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonpoint)'s "Double Stacked", [Green Velvet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Velvet)'s "[La La Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_La_Land_\(Green_Velvet_song\))", [Primal Scream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primal_Scream)'s "Don't Fight It, Feel It", [The Streets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Streets)' "[Blinded by the Lights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blinded_by_the_Lights)", [The Shamen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Shamen)'s "[Ebeneezer Goode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebeneezer_Goode)", [Missy Elliott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missy_Elliott)'s "[4 My People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_My_People)", [Pulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulp_\(band\))'s "[Sorted for E's & Wizz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorted_for_E's_&_Wizz)" and [Hot Chip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Chip)'s "[Over and Over](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over_and_Over)".[^cite_note-347]

## Research

See also: [List of investigational hallucinogens and entactogens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_investigational_hallucinogens_and_entactogens), [List of investigational post-traumatic stress disorder drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_investigational_post-traumatic_stress_disorder_drugs), and [Psychedelic therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_therapy)

MDMA is being investigated as a potential treatment for social impairments in [autism](29113700.md).[^cite_note-348]

The [British](19097669.md) [critical psychiatrist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critical_psychiatrist) [Joanna Moncrieff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joanna_Moncrieff) has critiqued the use and study of MDMA and related drugs like [psychedelics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_drug) for treatment of psychiatric disorders, highlighting concerns including excessive hype around these drugs, blurred lines between medical and recreational use, flawed clinical trial findings, financial conflicts of interest, strong expectancy effects and large placebo responses, short-term benefits over placebo, and their potential for adverse effects, among others.[^cite_note-Moncrieff2025-349]

Fig. 7 shows the mean PD effects of MDMA plotted against simultaneous plasma concentrations at the different time points (hysteresis loops). The increases in "any drug effect" (Fig. 7a) and MAP (Fig. 7b) returned to baseline within 6 h when MDMA concentrations were still high. This clockwise hysteresis indicates that a smaller MDMA effect was seen at a given plasma concentration later in time, indicating rapid acute pharmacodynamic tolerance, which was similarly described for cocaine \[33]. \[...] Figure 7. Pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic (PK-PD) relationship. MDMA effects are plotted against simultaneous MDMA plasma concentrations (a, b). The time of sampling is noted next to each point in minutes or hours after MDMA administration. The clockwise hysteresis indicates acute tolerance to the effects of MDMA.

[hdl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hdl_\(identifier\)):[1887/3515020](https://hdl.handle.net/1887%2F3515020). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [35878791](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35878791). In addition, the strong prior expectations that many people have about psychedelics directly contribute to the psychedelic experience and as a consequence it has been suggested that psychedelics may act as a 'super-placebo' (Hartogsohn, 2016). Specifically, strong prior expectations (e.g., that a specific intervention will likely trigger a mystical experience) will increase the likelihood of having e.g., a mystical-type experience (Maij et al., 2019), and this placebo-effect is further boosted by the psychedelic-induced suggestibility.

[^cite_note-Mendelson_2012-121]: &#x20;Mendelson J, Baggott MJ, Li L, Coyle J, Galloway GP (2012). "Poster Session II (PII 1-111): PII-41. MDMA-Induced Increases in Blood Pressure Are Not Mediated by α-Adrenergic Mechanisms and Are Not Due To Elevated Peripheral Vascular Resistance". *Clinical Pharmacology & Therapeutics*. **91** (S1 \[American Society for Clinical Pharmacology and Therapeutics Abstract of papers, 2012 Annual Meeting Gaylord National Hotel and Convention Center National Harbor, Maryland March 14–17, 2012]): S51–S93 (S66–S66). [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1038/clpt.2011.361](https://doi.org/10.1038%2Fclpt.2011.361). [ISSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISSN_\(identifier\)) [0009-9236](https://search.worldcat.org/issn/0009-9236). MDMA increased heart rate (HR) by 25 bpm (p<.001), \[cardiac output (CO)] by 1.75 L/min (p<0.01) but did not alter \[stroke volume (SV)] or \[systemic vascular resistance (SVR)]. Compared to MDMA alone the combination of MDMA + prazosin further increased HR by 24 bpm (p<0.001) and CO by 3.3L/min (p<0.02). MDMA increased systolic and diastolic blood pressure (SBP, DBP) by 26 mmHg (p<0.001 each); prazosin attenuated MDMA effects on DBP by 9.3 mmHg (p<001) but did not alter SBP. \[...] MDMA increases HR, producing elevations in CO. The hypertensive effects of MDMA are not due to elevated peripheral vascular resistance and the blood pressure effects of MDMA are not attenuated by α-adrenergic blockade, suggesting that MDMA may produce CV effects through non-α-adrenergic mechanisms.

[^cite_note-Simmler_2013-139]: &#x20;Simmler LD, Buser TA, Donzelli M, Schramm Y, Dieu LH, Huwyler J, et al. (January 2013). ["Pharmacological characterization of designer cathinones in vitro"](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC3572571). *British Journal of Pharmacology*. **168** (2): 458–470. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1111/j.1476-5381.2012.02145.x](https://doi.org/10.1111%2Fj.1476-5381.2012.02145.x). [PMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMC_\(identifier\)) [3572571](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC3572571). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [22897747](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22897747). β-Keto-analogue cathinones also exhibited approximately 10-fold lower affinity for the TA1 receptor compared with their respective non-β-keto amphetamines. \[...] Activation of TA1 receptors negatively modulates dopaminergic neurotransmission. Importantly, methamphetamine decreased DAT surface expression via a TA1 receptor-mediated mechanism and thereby reduced the presence of its own pharmacological target (Xie and Miller, 2009). MDMA and amphetamine have been shown to produce enhanced DA and 5-HT release and locomotor activity in TA1 receptor knockout mice compared with wild-type mice (Lindemann et al., 2008; Di Cara et al., 2011). Because methamphetamine and MDMA auto-inhibit their neurochemical and functional effects via TA1 receptors, low affinity for these receptors may result in stronger effects on monoamine systems by cathinones compared with the classic amphetamines.

[^cite_note-Docherty_2021-148]: &#x20;Docherty JR, Alsufyani HA (August 2021). ["Pharmacology of Drugs Used as Stimulants"](https://doi.org/10.1002%2Fjcph.1918). *Journal of Clinical Pharmacology*. **61** (Suppl 2): S53–S69. Receptor-mediated actions of amphetamine and other amphetamine derivatives \[...] may involve trace amine-associated receptors (TAARs) at which amphetamine and MDMA also have significant potency.85–87 Many stimulants have potency at the rat TAAR1 in the micromolar range but tend to be about 5 to 10 times less potent at the human TAAR1, \[...] Activation of the TAAR1 receptor causes inhibition of dopaminergic transmission in the mesocorticolimbic system, and TAAR1 agonists attenuated psychostimulant abuse-related behaviors.89 It is likely that TAARs contribute to the actions of specific stimulants to modulate dopaminergic, serotonergic, and glutamate signaling,90 and drugs acting on the TAAR1 may have therapeutic potential.91 In the periphery, stimulants such as MDMA and cathinone produce vasoconstriction, part of which may involve TAARs, although only relatively high concentrations produced vascular contractions resistant to a cocktail of monoamine antagonist drugs.86

[^cite_note-CaseyHeifets2025-154]: &#x20;Casey AB, Heifets BD (October 2025). ["History, pharmacology and therapeutic mechanisms of 3,4-methylenedioxymethamphetamine (MDMA)"](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12873472). *Br J Pharmacol*. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1111/bph.70225](https://doi.org/10.1111%2Fbph.70225). [PMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMC_\(identifier\)) [12873472](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12873472). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [41121647](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41121647). Unlike structurally related psychostimulants such as amphetamine and methamphetamine (Figure 1), which primarily release noradrenaline and dopamine over 5-HT via an identical mechanism, MDMA preferentially releases 5-HT and noradrenaline (Rothman et al., 2001) (Table 1). However, the selectivity by which MDMA releases monoamines is species-dependent. For example, in rat brain synaptosomes, MDMA preferentially releases 5-HT with modest selectivity (<10-fold) over noradrenaline and dopamine (Rothman et al., 2001; Setola et al., 2003). In contrast, studies using cloned human transporters in heterologous cells indicate that MDMA preferentially inhibits the release of noradrenaline with modest selectivity over 5-HT and dopamine (Han & Gu, 2006; Verrico et al., 2007). These species differences are most prominent for SERT, as MDMA more potently inhibits rodent over human SERT, with no species differences at NET and DAT (Han & Gu, 2006).

[^cite_note-Espinoza_2014-159]: &#x20;Espinoza S, Gainetdinov RR (2014). "Neuronal Functions and Emerging Pharmacology of TAAR1". *Taste and Smell*. Topics in Medicinal Chemistry. Vol. 23. Cham: Springer International Publishing. pp. 175–194. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1007/7355_2014_78](https://doi.org/10.1007%2F7355_2014_78). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-3-319-48925-4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-3-319-48925-4). Interestingly, the concentrations of amphetamine found to be necessary to activate TAAR1 are in line with what was found in drug abusers \[3, 51, 52]. Thus, it is likely that some of the effects produced by amphetamines could be mediated by TAAR1. Indeed, in a study in mice, MDMA effects were found to be mediated in part by TAAR1, in a sense that MDMA auto-inhibits its neurochemical and functional actions \[46]. Based on this and other studies (see other section), it has been suggested that TAAR1 could play a role in reward mechanisms and that amphetamine activity on TAAR1 counteracts their known behavioral and neurochemical effects mediated via dopamine neurotransmission.

[^cite_note-Kuropka_2023-160]: &#x20;Kuropka P, Zawadzki M, Szpot P (May 2023). "A narrative review of the neuropharmacology of synthetic cathinones-Popular alternatives to classical drugs of abuse". *Human Psychopharmacology*. **38** (3) e2866. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1002/hup.2866](https://doi.org/10.1002%2Fhup.2866). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [36866677](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36866677). Another feature that distinguishes \[synthetic cathinones (SCs)] from amphetamines is their negligible interaction with the trace amine associated receptor 1 (TAAR1). Activation of this receptor reduces the activity of dopaminergic neurones, thereby reducing psychostimulatory effects and addictive potential (Miller, 2011; Simmler et al., 2016). Amphetamines are potent agonists of this receptor, making them likely to self‐inhibit their stimulating effects. In contrast, SCs show negligible activity towards TAAR1 (Kolaczynska et al., 2021; Rickli et al., 2015; Simmler et al., 2014, 2016). \[...] It is worth noting, however, that for TAAR1 there is considerable species variability in its interaction with ligands, and it is possible that the in vitro activity of \[rodent TAAR1 agonists] may not translate into activity in the human body (Simmler et al., 2016). The lack of self‐regulation by TAAR1 may partly explain the higher addictive potential of SCs compared to amphetamines (Miller, 2011; Simmler et al., 2013).

[^cite_note-Baumann_2008-172]: &#x20;Baumann MH, Clark RD, Rothman RB (August 2008). ["Locomotor stimulation produced by 3,4-methylenedioxymethamphetamine (MDMA) is correlated with dialysate levels of serotonin and dopamine in rat brain"](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC2491560). *Pharmacology, Biochemistry, and Behavior*. **90** (2): 208–217. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1016/j.pbb.2008.02.018](https://doi.org/10.1016%2Fj.pbb.2008.02.018). [PMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMC_\(identifier\)) [2491560](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC2491560). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [18403002](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18403002). Determining the role of specific 5-HT receptors in MDMA's effects in vivo is complicated by the presence of 14 different 5-HT receptor subtypes (Barnes and Sharp, 1999; Hoyer et al., 2002), many of which affect DA function (Alex and Pehek, 2007; Bubar and Cunningham, 2006). Nonetheless, there is general agreement that 5-HT1B, 5-HT2A and 5-HT2C receptor subtypes influence locomotor effects of MDMA. Hyperactivity produced by MDMA is mimicked by administration of the 5-HT1B agonist RU-24969 (Rempel et al., 1993), and 5-HT1B antagonists inhibit MDMA-induced ambulation (Callaway and Geyer, 1992b; Fletcher et al., 2002; McCreary et al., 1999). 5-HT2A antagonists reduce ambulation produced by MDMA (Fletcher et al., 2002; Kehne et al., 1996), whereas 5-HT2C antagonists markedly enhance it (Bankson and Cunningham, 2002; Fletcher et al., 2006). Taken together, these data reveal that 5-HT1B and 5-HT2A receptors facilitate, while 5-HT2C receptors suppress, forward locomotion evoked by MDMA administration. Importantly, the neural circuits underlying serotonergic modulation of MDMA-induced activity are largely unexplored.

MDA, the active metabolite of MDMA (Hysek et al. 2011), shows a tenfold higher potency for 5-HT2A agonism than MDMA (Rickli et al. 2015c). MDA likely contributes to the mode of action of MDMA and might contribute to the mild hallucinogenic effects of MDMA.

Racemic \[MDA] and S-(+)-MDA reportedly induce WDS in monkeys and rats, respectively (Schlemmer and Davis 1986; Hiramatsu et al. 1989). Although \[MDMA] does not induce the HTR in mice, both of the stereoisomers of MDMA have been shown to elicit the response (Fantegrossi et al. 2004, 2005b). 5-HT depletion inhibits the response to S-(+)-MDMA but does not alter the response to R-(−)-MDMA, suggesting the isomers act through different mechanisms (Fantegrossi et al. 2005b). This suggestion is consistent with the fact that S-(+)- and R-(−)-MDMA exhibit qualitatively distinct pharmacological profiles, with the S-(+)isomer working primarily as a monoamine releaser (Johnson et al. 1986; Baumann et al. 2008; Murnane et al. 2010) and the R-(−)-enantiomer acting directly through 5-HT2A receptors (Lyon et al. 1986; Nash et al. 1994). In contrast to their effects in mice, Hiramatsu reported that S-(+)- and R-(−)-MDMA fail to produce WDS in rats (Hiramatsu et al. 1989). The discrepant findings with MDMA in mice and rats may reflect species differences in sensitivity to the HTR (see below for further discussion).

Serotonergic psychedelics will cause a rapid back and forth head movement in mice. The potency measured in the HTR assay has been shown to correlate very well with the human potencies of psychedelics.18 Neither R-MDMA or \[...] produced any head twitches at all doses tested, suggesting that neither has high hallucinogenic potential.

Notably, in a study by Rickli and colleagues, MDMA did not activate the 5-HT2B receptor in the functional assay at investigated concentrations (EC50 > 20 μM); however, \[MDA], the main psychoactive N-demethylated phase I metabolite of MDMA, potently activated the receptor at submicromolar concentrations \[14]. This suggests that the metabolite MDA rather than MDMA itself may lead to valvulopathy and that there could be a significant metabolic contribution to MDMA-induced effects and adverse effect.

A controlled study on eight experienced MDMA users reported that 1.5 mg/kg (comparable to what was deemed a typical dosage amount) consumed orally resulted in the subjective effects peaking within 2 h of ingestion (Harris et al., 2002). Other research indicates effects to emerge between 20 and 60 min, with them peaking between 60 and 90 min and lasting up to 5 h (Green et al., 2003). A dose of 100 mg has a half-life of 8–9h(De la Torre et al., 2004), although as mentioned above, users are unaware of the dose they ingest.

Metabolism does not appear to be altered by chronic exposure, thus dose escalation appears to arise from pharmacodynamic rather than pharmacokinetic tolerance \[24]. \[...] The terminal plasma half-life of methamphetamine of approximately 10 hours is similar across administration routes, but with substantial inter-individual variability. Acute effects persist for up to 8 hours following a single moderate dose of 30 mg \[30]. \[...] peak plasma methamphetamine concentration occurs after 4 hours \[35]. Nevertheless, peak cardiovascular and subjective effects occur rapidly (within 5–15 minutes). The dissociation between peak plasma concentration and clinical effects indicates acute tolerance, which may reflect rapid molecular processes such as redistribution of vesicular monoamines and internalization of monoamine receptors and transporters \[6,36]. Acute subjective effects diminish over 4 hours, while cardiovascular effects tend to remain elevated. This is important, as the marked acute tachyphylaxis to subjective effects may drive repeated use within intervals of 4 hours, while cardiovascular risks may increase \[11,35].

[^cite_note-Abbas_2024-218]: &#x20;Abbas K, Barnhardt EW, Nash PL, Streng M, Coury DL (April 2024). ["A review of amphetamine extended release once-daily options for the management of attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder"](https://doi.org/10.1080%2F14737175.2024.2321921). *Expert Review of Neurotherapeutics*. **24** (4): 421–432. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1080/14737175.2024.2321921](https://doi.org/10.1080%2F14737175.2024.2321921). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [38391788](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38391788). For several decades, clinical benefits of amphetamines have been limited by the pharmacologic half-life of around 4 hours. Although higher doses can produce higher maximum concentrations, they do not affect the half-life of the dose. Therefore, to achieve longer durations of effect, stimulants had to be dosed at least twice daily. Further, these immediate-release doses were found to have their greatest effect shortly after administration, with a rapid decline in effect after reaching peak blood concentrations. The clinical correlation of this was found in comparing math problems attempted and solved between a mixed amphetamine salts preparation (MAS) 10 mg once at 8 am vs 8 am followed by 12 pm \[14]. The study also demonstrated the phenomenon of acute tolerance, where even if blood concentrations were maintained over the course of the day, clinical efficacy in the form of math problems attempted and solved would diminish over the course of the day. These findings eventually led to the development of a once daily preparation (MAS XR) \[15], which is a composition of 50% immediate-release beads and 50% delayed release beads intended to mimic this twice-daily dosing with only a single administration.

[^cite_note-van_Gaalen_2019-219]: &#x20;van Gaalen MM, Schlumbohm C, Folgering JH, Adhikari S, Bhattacharya C, Steinbach D, et al. (April 2019). ["Development of a Semimechanistic Pharmacokinetic-Pharmacodynamic Model Describing Dextroamphetamine Exposure and Striatal Dopamine Response in Rats and Nonhuman Primates following a Single Dose of Dextroamphetamine"](https://doi.org/10.1124%2Fjpet.118.254508). *The Journal of Pharmacology and Experimental Therapeutics*. **369** (1): 107–120. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1124/jpet.118.254508](https://doi.org/10.1124%2Fjpet.118.254508). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_\(identifier\)) [30733244](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30733244). Acute tolerance has been demonstrated for methamphetamine in rats (Segal and Kuczenski, 2006), and for D-amphetamine in rats (Lewander, 1971), \[non-human primates (NHPs)] (Jedema et al., 2014) and humans (Angrist et al., 1987; Brauer et al., 1996; Dolder et al., 2017). In vivo measurement of dopamine by microdialysis was used in rats and NHPs to evaluate these time-dependent effects. In humans, various subjective measures of mood related to the drug's euphoric effects were observed to decline more rapidly than plasma concentrations following D-amphetamine oral doses ranging from 20 to 40 mg (Angrist et al., 1987; Brauer et al., 1996; Dolder et al., 2017). Whereas peak plasma concentrations and subjective effects occurred between 2 and 4 hours following administration, drug effect measures had largely returned to baseline values by 8 hours despite continued exposure to the drug (mean half-life=8 hours following a 40 mg dose (Dolder et al., 2017)).

[^cite_note-Bisagno_2021-220]: &#x20;Bisagno V, Cadet JL (2021). "Methamphetamine and MDMA Neurotoxicity: Biochemical and Molecular Mechanisms". *Handbook of Neurotoxicity*. Cham: Springer International Publishing. pp. 1–24. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1007/978-3-030-71519-9_80-1](https://doi.org/10.1007%2F978-3-030-71519-9_80-1). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-3-030-71519-9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-3-030-71519-9). Injections of large doses of MDMA cause massive release of 5-HT from presynaptic vesicles, followed by a rapid decrease in 5-HT and 5-hydroxyindoleacetic acid (5-HIAA) levels and decreased TPH activity (Górska et al., 2018; Lyles & Cadet, 2003). There do not appear to be losses of 5-HT uptake sites at early time points after MDMA administration (Lyles & Cadet, 2003). \[...] MDMA also perturbs the function of SERT (Green et al., 2003), a marker of the integrity of serotonin neurons (Blakely et al., 1994). By virtue of its moderating synaptic 5-HT levels, SERT is crucial for the process of 5-HT neurotransmission (Green et al., 2003). MDMA downregulates SERT function without altering SERT mRNA or protein expression, and this rapid downregulation is sustained for at least 90 min and is dose-dependent (Kivell et al., 2010).
